# Open decisions for Daniel

Items parked here need Daniel's call. Work continues elsewhere until he
answers; each item notes the assumption currently built into the code.

| # | Decision | Assumption in code until decided | Raised |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | License stays MIT, or switch to Apache-2.0 / GPL for stronger copyleft? | MIT | 2026-10-07 |
| 2 | App name: "Plan Studio" is a working title. Keep it, or pick a brand name? | Plan Studio | 2026-10-07 |

| 3 | Chief library content (.calib/.calibz): Plan Studio will read Core/Bonus/Manufacturer catalogs from the local Chief install at runtime (licensed to Daniel), never bundling them in the repo. OK, or should imported items be re-saved into a user library folder outside the repo? | Read at runtime from Chief's folders; our own starter symbols ship in the repo | 2026-10-08 |
| 4 | Hotkeys off macOS: Daniel's Control+Z (Down One Floor) and Command+Z (Undo) fold onto one Ctrl+Z on Windows/Linux. Code lets the Command chord win (Undo = Ctrl+Z, Chief's Windows default) and reports Down One Floor as unmapped there. Pick a different Windows key for Down One Floor? | Undo wins; Down One Floor unmapped off macOS | 2026-10-08 |
| 5 | Straight Railing's Chief hotkey is ⌘Q, which is Quit on macOS. Shown on the entry, not bound. Rebind (e.g. ⌥⌘Q) or leave? | Not bound | 2026-10-08 |
| 6 | Click Stairs heading: built as "the pointer's last movement direction before the click" (defaults to up the screen). Verify in Chief. Alt reverses the arrow (p. 778) and Ctrl/Cmd stops the wall snaps (pp. 777, 780). | As built | 2026-10-08 |

| 8 | ~~3D selection tint and billboards go through `plan-view3d`'s whole-scene upload~~ **Resolved 2026-10-08:** `Viewport3d::set_overlay` holds meshes that are drawn after the cached scene (selection tint, hover tint, billboards); changing them uploads only those meshes and leaves the cached scene, the camera framing and the shadow map alone. Overlay meshes cast no shadows (a billboard no longer shadows the ground), and the tint is a translucent copy of the mesh with the edge lines drawn over it, not an outline pass. Draw a real outline? | Overlay, no outline | 2026-10-08 |

Shift the centerline to match? | Main layer kept in place (round 13): the centerline shifts by twice the main layer's offset and the ends of joined walls follow; set back to the plain exterior-side flip if dragging neighbours is unwelcome | 2026-10-08 |

| 17 | ~~Painted materials show as the closest scene material~~ **Resolved 2026-10-08:** `plan_3d::Mesh.color` (exact sRGB) is the albedo of a painted mesh in the viewport and the ray tracer (read as sRGB and converted to linear light, as bitmaps are, so a flat colour and a bitmap of the same colour match); the mesh keeps the scene material that stands for the library material (`scene_material`) only for its gloss, metal and clear glass. A library material with an image file (Material Builder) or a generated texture also gets its own bitmap per mesh in the viewport (`Viewport3d::set_surface_textures`, loaded through the texture store). Still open: the ray tracer draws the flat colour, not the bitmap, of a painted material; a bitmap is not tinted by the colour. Bind the bitmap in the ray tracer too? | Colour on the mesh, bitmap in the viewport | 2026-10-08 |

| 44 | Schedule numbers (manual p. 715; changed in Round 16 brief 04, was: Renumber Schedule wrote the marks into `Opening.schedule_number`). A schedule keeps the numbers of its objects in `Schedule::numbers` (kind, floor, id, number). A new schedule starts with the objects there are, in ascending order of their Label cell (ties by reading order across the plan); an object the schedule has no number for yet goes below the last number, in the order the objects were placed (ids ascend as objects are made); a deleted object leaves a gap; an edit never moves a row. Renumber Schedule closes the gaps and keeps the order, and writes no mark into the openings. Move Row, Move Up / Down in Schedule renumber the rows between. A schedule saved before Round 16 has no record and keeps numbering by reading order until its first Renumber or move. Rooms and framing lines have no id, so they stay in listing order. | Records kept in the schedule | 2026-10-08 |
| 45 | Three or more walls ending at one point (W-34, W-103): the most nearly collinear pair (a bend of at most 30 degrees) runs through with square ends and every other wall butts the near face of the through walls, each face of the butting wall meeting the face of the through wall on its side, so faces step where the through walls differ in thickness (the butting end is then cut slightly slanted). When several pairs qualify (a `+`, or the halves of a cut crossing) the pair with the better wall wins: exterior over interior, then thicker, then earlier in the wall list. With no near-collinear pair (a `Y`) neighbours in angular order are mitered pairwise. The brief's "thickest/exterior wall runs through" is read this way because a wall that only ends at the point cannot run through it; whether Chief prefers priority over collinearity is to be confirmed. | Collinear pair first, priority breaks ties | 2026-10-08 |
| 46 | Through Wall At Start / At End (W-39): the flagged wall runs past the corner with a square end flush with the other wall's far face (the extension is half the other wall's width projected on the flagged wall), and the other wall butts its near face. A flagged end beats the exterior/thickness priority at a junction; two flagged ends fall back to the priority. Not drawn in 3D yet (straight walls are boxes to their centerline ends there). | Plan outline only | 2026-10-08 |

Rotate to Align turns about the first point to the nearest axis (never more than 45 degrees); Chief may ask for a target line instead (verify in Chief). Locked underlays and pictures on locked layers refuse. | Chief's exact gestures unknown | 2026-10-08 |
| 104 | PDF underlays (round 14, L-46): there is no rasterizer, so a PDF is usable when its pages are pictures. The reader scans the file for `N G obj` instead of trusting the cross-reference table, unpacks `/ObjStm`, walks the page tree (inherited `/Resources`, form XObjects) and takes the largest image of each page; JPEG streams are kept, Flate pictures become PNG files under `~/.plan-studio/underlays/`, CCITT / JBIG2 / JPX / LZW and vector pages give the "export the page as a PNG" message. A page's soft mask and clipping are ignored. | PDF rasterizer | 2026-10-08 |
| 105 | Detail Components catalogue (round 14): real lumber sizes (2x4 1 1/2" x 3 1/2" to 2x12 1 1/2" x 11 1/4"), batt cavities 3 1/2", 5 1/2", 7 1/4", 2" rigid, three flashings drawn as a heavy line with a thin second line, 7/16" OSB and 5/8" plywood, lap / board and batten / shiplap siding profiles, 1/2" x 10" and 5/8" x 12" anchor bolts. Manual correction (pp. 1323-1327): Chief's Terrain Specification panels are General, Contours, Polyline, Spline, Selected Line/Arc, Line Style, Fill Style, Materials, Label, Object Information and Schedule; the pad is the Flatten Pad switch. Chief's import dialog is not captured; verify in Chief. It is a form of the Terrain Specification dialog opened from Terrain > Site Objects, and OK stores the points as one "Terrain Specification" undo step (only when points were added). Manual correction (pp. 1342-1346): Chief imports terrain through two assistants under File > Import; only way points carry elevation, route points are ignored and track points become markers, polylines or a perimeter. Chief's retaining-wall behavior is not captured; verify in Chief. Defaults: walls, floors and roofs are built, ceiling framing is off (it does not exist in earlier plans), auto rebuild is off, nothing is retained, the framing layers are turned on after a build. | 114 | Framing round 14, plan line styles of built framing by member kind (`framing_view::plan_style`): wall members solid with a light fill, wall blocking lighter, floor joists and rims solid, ceiling joists dashed (6 / 4 points), rafters, ridge, hips, valleys, fascia and truss chords long-dashed (10 / 5), truss webs dotted (2 / 3). Chief's documented line styles for framing are not captured; verify in Chief. | As built | 2026-10-08 |
| 115 | Framing round 14, floor and ceiling joists: Open Below (a room with no floor platform) gets no floor joists and the room wholly under it no ceiling joists; a room with no ceiling gets no ceiling joists; the joists bear on every wall unless the Floor tab says "Exterior walls and Bearing Lines" (then only exterior walls enclose a platform and Bearing Lines split it). Chief marks bearing walls another way that is not captured; verify in Chief. Span checks (`plan_framing::span`) are a beam calculation for Douglas Fir-Larch No. 2 (bending and live-load deflection, size and repetitive factors, 40/10 psf floors, 10/5 psf ceilings, 20/10 psf roofs), a planning aid that sits a little under the IRC tables and ignores shear and bearing; it is not Chief's structural calculator. | As built | 2026-10-08 |
| 116 | Roof Styles (round 14, RF-3, RF-4): Build Roof has Hip, Gable, Shed, Gambrel, Dutch Gable and Half Hip buttons that write the roof directive of every exterior wall on the footprint before building (one undo step). The ridge runs along the longest edge: Gable and Dutch make the walls across it Full Gable / Dutch Gable Walls; Shed makes the eave wall furthest back (smallest y) the High Shed/Gable Wall and the ends Full Gable; Gambrel gives the eaves 18 in 12 below a break half way in from the eave and 6 in 12 above (`GAMBREL_PITCHES`). Mansard and Flat are not presets (a mansard is the gambrel's upper pitch on all four walls; Flat is the room's Flat Roof setting). Chief's list and numbers are unverified; verify in Chief. Differs by design: in Chief the Roof Styles panel only links to online tutorials, one per style (manual p. 834); the preset buttons are a Plan Studio convenience on top of the wall Roof panel (pp. 407, 826), which stays Chief's real mechanism. | Written to the walls | 2026-10-08 |
| 117 | Half hip (round 14, RF-3): a Full Gable wall that has an Upper Pitch is a half hip: the pitch is the small hip's and Starts at Height is where it begins (the clip), default 60% of the gable's height. The hip starts on the rake line (gable edge plus its overhang), so the end wall rises vertically to the clip and the hip face slopes back to a shortened ridge. Chief's Half Hip may differ (verify in Chief). | Upper Pitch on a gable wall | 2026-10-08 |
| 118 | Wings (round 14, RF-9): a floor's pitched rooms are grouped by plate height (the lowest exterior wall beside the room, so a shared wall drawn at the main height does not lift a garage) and each group is roofed on its own footprint at its own plate; the footprint is the outline of the union of the group's rooms. First-floor rooms the floors up to the build floor do not cover (under 5% covered) are roofed on their own floor, stored in that floor's roof slot without settings (the build floor owns the settings; Auto Rebuild watches the walls of the floors below). An edge that has a taller wall or an upper floor outside it is a high shed with no overhang. A room only partly under the floor above gets no roof below (it would have to wrap the upper floor), and a floor with Flat Roof or Roof Over off rooms keeps its one footprint at the tallest wall. Foundation floors, floors below grade and deck/half walls are not wings or plates. Manual correction (p. 826, R-114, Round 16): Chief groups buildings by a Roof Group number the user sets on rooms (`RoomName::roof_group`, Room Specification > General > Options) and otherwise roofs the whole exterior as one integrated system. A room in a non-default group goes with the other rooms of that group whatever its plate height, and the group is roofed on its own footprint as one integrated system at the tallest plate of its rooms; plate-height grouping stays only for the rooms left in the default group 0, as Plan Studio's own rule for a garage beside a house (see RB1). The group is part of the roof signature, so Auto Rebuild notices a change. | Per plate height | 2026-10-08 |
| 119 | Extend Slope Downward to the wall below (round 14, RF-24): a wall with no typed drop reaches down to the top of the nearest lower-floor exterior wall within 6 ft of the edge (at most 20 ft); with none below it drops 24 in as before. | 127 | Material class (round 14, C-61): the class holds the sliders in the range that makes it true (Plastic not metallic and glossy, Metal at least 80% metallic, Glass at least 50% clear and smooth, Mirror fully metallic, opaque and smooth, Emissive glowing at least 30%, Transparent at least 20% clear) and picking a class moves the sliders to its typical values; `MaterialDef::surface()` is what both renderers read. | 128 | Painted-surface table (round 14): a painted mesh's roughness, metalness, transparency and glow reach the GL view and the ray tracer through `plan_3d::surface`, a process-wide table keyed by object id, scene material and exact colour, because `Mesh` and `Scene` are built by struct literal in many files and a new field would break them. Entries are set by `tools::materials::apply_overrides` and are never found by a mesh that is not painted that way. |
| 129 | Materials Defaults (round 14, C-60): the plan keeps one entry per object class and part (`Project::material_defaults`; classes Wall, Door, Window, Cabinet, Roof and Room); a mesh takes its own paint first, then its class default, then the built-in material. A whole-class entry (part empty) leaves a window's glass clear like a whole-object paint. Room Floor Finish and Ceiling Finish entries are also written to every floor's `FloorSettings` default surface materials so new rooms start with them. |
| 130 | Texture offset, angle and blend colour (round 14): they are baked into the bitmap the 3D view uploads (`plan_materials::transform_rgba`): the offset wraps, multiples of 90 degrees are exact, other angles resample with wrap-around and may show a faint seam at tile edges; the shader has no texture rotation uniform. Bump maps are not read yet. | Manual note (p. 1122): consistent with the manual, which lists Bump, Normal and Ambient Occlusion maps; this is a gap, not a conflict.
| 131 | Materials List by surface (round 14): it adds the areas of both faces of every wall (net of openings), the door or window of every opening, each room's floor and ceiling (interior area) and each roof plane's sloped area, per material, with the Materials List tab's price and unit; a wall shared by two rooms counts its whole length for each room; a room shows a material only when it was painted, the floor's default surfaces name one or the Room class default does (there is no built-in room finish). A linear-foot price has no area quantity and shows 0. | Manual correction (p. 1105): wall materials applied with the Material Painter are cosmetic and are not calculated (apart from foundation wall footings), and the list counts objects by their center point. Symbols have no named parts, so the Symbol Specification's Materials tab paints the whole object. |

| 134 | Chief `.plan` import, stage 3: the 0.875" floor finish offset (round 14): a flight's `base` is `bottom - 0.875 - floor elevation` (snapped to 0 under an inch), because every stair read starts 0.875" above its floor's nominal elevation and ends 0.875" above the next one, while Plan Studio's stairs start at the floor elevation. A landing is at the base of the flight that leaves it. The offset is read as a floor finish (7/8"); verify in Chief whether it is a setting of the floor structure. | base 0 for every flight | 2026-10-08 |
| 135 | Chief `.plan` import, stage 3: roof outlines are rotated so the eave edge is first, and `overhang` is the measured baseline-to-eave distance (round 14): `plan_roof::classify_edges` takes `polygon3d[0] -> [1]` as the eave. The outline already includes the overhang, the baseline sits on the wall, and no overhang number is stored in the file, so the plane's `overhang` is the distance between the two (12" in 30 of the 95 planes with an eave past the baseline). | 163 | Backdrop picture (round 14, C-70): the camera stores only a file name (or a full path); the picture is read from `~/Documents/Chief Architect Premier X18 Data/Backdrops` (or `PLAN_STUDIO_BACKDROPS`) when a view needs it, shrunk to 2048 px on the long side, and drawn by the sky pass behind the model scaled to cover the view (centred, overflow cropped). | 164 | 3D > Lighting (round 14, C-62, C-63, C-64): the plan keeps one sun (direction clockwise from north, height, strength), an ambient level and one switch for every interior light (`Project.lighting`; off leaves the plan's lights and the electrical fixtures out of the live view and the ray tracer without deleting them). The default 225 degrees / 45 degrees is the viewport's old fixed key light. Preview is Low quality with no shadows or occlusion; Final View is the default look (Medium quality, DECISIONS 28), not High, so a camera does not make the view slower than before; Shading > Quality still raises it. | 165 | Walkthrough key frames and recording (round 14, C-71): a path node is a key frame carrying eye height, look direction, tilt and a hold in seconds; playback and recording walk each segment at the walking speed and stand still for the holds, and recording spreads its frames evenly in time so the last frame is the end of the walk. Line views are the layout renderer's vector lines rasterised (pen 0.25 mm thin / 0.5 mm heavy at the DPI, 1 px per 1700 px of width in pixel mode); the background is white or, for PNG and TIFF, transparent. The 3D view is ray traced from the viewport's camera at the chosen size (up to 4096 px) because the GL viewport cannot be read back, so it shows the default sun and sky (the same limit as Print Image of the 3D view). JPEG is a baseline YCbCr 4:4:4 encoder written for this (quality 10-100); BMP is 32-bit; TIFF is uncompressed RGBA. | 454 | Spell check word list (round 15, TXT-21): nothing is bundled. The list is read at run time from /usr/share/dict/words (or american-english, british-english, /usr/dict/words) on a background thread; Windows has none, so only the built-in design vocabulary and ~/.plan-studio/dictionary.txt count (the dialog says so). Because Webster's list has base forms only, a word also passes when stripping a common ending (s, es, ies, ves, ed, ing, er, est, ly, ness, ment, less, ful) or one common prefix leaves a listed word. Skipped: words with digits, capitals up to four letters (acronyms), internal capitals, one letter words, addresses and `<b>` markup tags; longer words in capitals are checked (plan notes are often in capitals). Suggestions: listed words within two edits (swaps count one), nearest first, plus a near stem with the word's ending, in the case of the misspelling. Ignore All lasts until the next check starts; Add appends to ~/.plan-studio/dictionary.txt. | OS list + endings + built-in design words | 2026-10-08 |

| 468 | Terrain round 15, import assistants (CB-569..CB-577; supersedes the GPX rule of row 107): the Import Terrain Assistant reads the column order (a Detect choice keeps the old per-line guess), filters by X, Y and Z ranges and thins to N evenly spread points (one point per square cell, cell size by bisection), then converts units per axis, maps a file point to the origin, scales the relief and rotates north counterclockwise; it creates a perimeter around the data when there is none. The GPS assistant reads way points (elevation), track points (no elevation: marker, polyline or perimeter) and ignores route points; a GPS marker is a 12" circle with the point's name on the Site Plan layer, a polyline a CAD polyline. The one-form importer (`import_points`) now reads way points only. The DXF layer conversion of the Import Drawing Assistant (CB-576) is not built. | As built; verify in Chief | 2026-10-08 |
| 469 | Terrain round 15, object panels (CB-527, CB-545, CB-546, CB-560, CB-568): the Label, Object Information and Schedule panels' data is `ObjectExtras`, stored on the object (features, breaks, walls, landscape objects, roads, elevation lines, the perimeter) or, for elevation points, regions and modifiers, in `Terrain.side_extras` keyed `point:i`, `region:i`, `modifier:i` and re-indexed when an element is removed (`remove_terrain_element`). Labels draw on the `Terrain Labels` layer; the Terrain Labels tool switches one on or off with a click. Terrain objects are scheduled under Chief's categories in the General schedule (no new schedule kind); the Schedule panel reassigns a category. | As built; verify in Chief | 2026-10-08 |
| 470 | Terrain round 15, roads (CB-566, CB-581..CB-588, CB-593..CB-595): Polyline Road, Driveway and Sidewalk, Median and Cul-de-sac are `RoadStrip`s with a closed `outline` (a cul-de-sac keeps its center and radius; 36 corners). A Median is a patch of the terrain material laid over the road, inside a curb when a road under it has one, not a hole cut in the road's mesh. A flare is a quarter-circle fillet of the given radius at the start or end of a strip. A driveway or sidewalk leaves a gap in the curb it crosses (`cut_curb`). Auto Generate Sidewalk puts sidewalks 48" wide beside both edges of the road and of every road joined to it at the offset typed; the Left/Right/All Connected dialog is not built. The old Polyline Road, Driveway and Sidewalk entries are renamed Straight. | As built; verify in Chief | 2026-10-08 |
| 471 | Terrain round 15, plants (CB-604, CB-605, CB-613..CB-616; refines row 110): a plant run can be drawn as a plant image (`Landscape.image`: file, size, aspect lock, elevation reference, center, reverse, faces camera, copyright, transparent color, four seasonal looks); in 3D the run is crossed billboards tinted for `Terrain.season`, narrower when the season's foliage share is low. Grow All Plants sets size = mature size x growth fraction (linear from the start fraction at planting to 1 at maturity; maturity is 20, 7 or 3 years by mature height) for runs that have a mature height; the Plant Chooser stores the catalog size as the mature size. A Garden Bed's Distributed Plant lattice (staggered rows, edge margin) is drawn in plan and 3D. A Sprinkler Line is a 2D dashed pipe, nothing in 3D. | As built; verify in Chief | 2026-10-08 |
| XL1 | Custom properties (Excel round trip): values are stored in one project-level table `Project.props` (`plan_core::props::PropTable`: the definitions plus a `BTreeMap<String, PropValue>` of values per object key) rather than a `props` slot on each object type. Opening, wall and symbol are plain structs, but cabinets, stairs, devices, roof records and framing members are opaque JSON, and ~20 builders share the model; one table works for every kind the same way, snapshots with the plan (undo is free) and adds one `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if)]` field. An object with no stored value shows the property's default; a value equal to the default is not stored. | Project side table keyed by object id | 2026-10-08 |
| XL2 | Property object keys: ids are project-unique for walls, doors, windows, cabinets, symbols, stairs, roof records and framing records (`door:12`, `window:7`, ...). Electrical devices number from 1 on every floor (`device:<floor>:<id>`); rooms have no id, so a room is `room:<floor>:<x>,<y>` by its centre rounded to whole inches and is found again within 6" if the walls moved a little. Floor indexes shift when a floor is inserted or deleted, which orphans device and room values (not lost, just unattached). Copy, paste and duplicate do not carry property values yet (queued). | Rooms and devices keyed by floor index | 2026-10-08 |
| XL3 | Excel exchange columns: which built-in fields write back: door/window Mark (sets `schedule_number`; blank returns to the automatic mark), Manufacturer, Model, Supplier, Comment, Description, ID, U-Factor, SHGC; room Name, Floor Finish, Ceiling Finish, Ceiling height (blank clears the override); cabinet Label; device Label, Circuit, Mount Height; fixture/furniture/plant Name; every custom property. Walls, stairs and framing take custom properties only. Everything computed (sizes, areas, counts, wall numbers) is shaded and locked in the sheet, and an edit to it is ignored with a note on import. | Short, safe list | 2026-10-08 |
| XL4 | Import diff: the `_meta` sheet keeps the exported text of every cell (the baseline). A cell is an edit only if it differs from its baseline, so a mark that renumbered after a deletion, or an area that was recomputed, is not read as an edit. If the plan's value also changed since the export the change is flagged as a conflict in the review dialog (Excel's value wins unless unchecked). A workbook or CSV without `_meta` compares each cell with the plan's current value and reads columns by heading. | Three-way diff via `_meta` | 2026-10-08 |
| XL5 | Sheet protection has no password (Review > Unprotect Sheet opens it up) so a locked column is a guard rail, not security. List-type and Yes/No properties get a drop-down; a list whose choices contain a comma or quote, or run past Excel's 255-character literal, gets no drop-down (the import still checks the value). | No password; literal lists only | 2026-10-08 |
| XL6 | Properties tab: added by the shared `SpecDialog` frame (thread-local session armed by the host) to every dialog of a kind that has at least one property, and to no other, so a plan with no properties looks exactly as before. OK applies the dialog and the tab as one undo step (the property edit joins the dialog's step; if only the tab changed the step is 'Object Properties'). An invalid entry blocks OK like any other field. Dialogs hosted outside `SpecDialogs` (wall and opening in `main.rs`, room in `build_tools`) arm their own session. | Tab appears only when the kind has properties | 2026-10-08 |
| XL7 | Workbook watch: after an export the plan polls the file's modification time every 2 s from the UI frame (no thread) and shows a small 'Workbook changed - import?' bar anchored above the status bar (not a status-bar slot; the bar's layout has none to spare) with Import and Dismiss. The watch ends when another plan is opened or saved under another name. Export for Editing with no schedule placed exports the standard eight (door, window, room, wall, cabinet, electrical, fixture, stair) over the whole plan. | Floating bar above the status bar | 2026-10-08 |
| IA1 | Opening placement rules live in plan-core (`openings/placement.rs`), integration pass 2: the tools, `Project::slide_opening`, `ops::place_opening_at` and the Specification dialog's neighbour check share one rule set (2 in clear between jambs, none between two windows, 2 in off the body of a wall that meets or crosses the host, 2 in from the wall ends). A slide may leave a zone it is already inside but never go deeper (an old plan with an opening beside a junction can still move). `add_opening` keeps its old end rule only (tests and the Chief importer place openings right at corners). Differs from Chief only where Chief's 2 in is unverified: verify in Chief. |
| IA2 | Bay Roof options (Options tab of Bay, Box and Bow windows; DW-48, DW-167, RF-29): `OpeningSpec.bay_roof` = kind (Default for Style, None, Flat, Shed, Hip), pitch (default 6:12) and overhang (default 0). Default keeps the old look: hip over a bay or bow, shed over a box window. Flat ignores pitch and overhang. Chief's Use Existing Roof / Extend Existing Roof Over / Rectangular Roof Over are not built. Verify in Chief. |
| IA3 | Room-mode paint reaches the 3D plates by role, not by keyword: a surface a room names itself (`RoomName.floor_finish`, `ceiling_finish`, `misc.wall_covering`) always gets a thin plate, tagged with an object id that has the high bit set (`plan_3d::room_surface_id`); `tools::materials::apply_overrides` repaints those plates with the exact library material. Rooms that only inherit the floor's default keep the keyword table and no tag. |
| IA4 | A window placed into a bedroom (any room whose type `CodeMinimums::is_sleeping` accepts, either side of the wall) starts at least as large and as low as `defaults.code.bedroom_window` (width and height are raised, the sill lowered); projecting windows and other rooms are untouched. Build Foundation's footing starts from `defaults.code.footing_width` and `footing_thickness`, which equal the old 16 x 8 until Apply code minimums changes them. |
| LB1 | Material packages (Lightbeans): the file names inside a Lightbeans zip and the keys of `productmetadata.txt` are not published and no sample zip was on disk, so detection is pattern-tolerant: map names match case-insensitively on substrings (albedo, basecolor, base_color, diffuse, color; normal, nrm, nor; rough; metal; height, disp, displacement, bump; ao, ambient, occlusion; opacity, alpha), previews and glossiness are ignored, a GL normal map wins over a DirectX one (a DX-only map has its green flipped), the largest file wins when a zip holds several sizes. Metadata is `key: value` or `key=value` lines; width / height / size keys with mm, cm, m, in or ft in the value or the key; a number with no unit at all is read as millimetres. Verify with a real Lightbeans zip. | Pattern-tolerant detection; bare numbers are mm | 2026-10-08 |
| LB2 | Imported package files are copied (never moved) to `~/.plan-studio/textures/<package name>/` as `albedo.jpg`, `normal.png` ... with a `package.json` (source zip, import date, size); the material is saved to My Materials in the category `Lightbeans` with class General, the maker as manufacturer, and `texture_path` plus `normal_map`, `roughness_map`, `metallic_map`, `height_map`, `ao_map`, `opacity_map` pointing at the copies. Re-importing a package of the same name overwrites its folder and its material. Plan Studio never downloads, bundles or caches Lightbeans files in the repository or in a plan. | Copy to the user textures folder | 2026-10-08 |
| LB3 | Rendering: surfaces use the planar mapping, so the tangent frame follows from the face normal (`plan_materials::pbr::tangent_frame`, copied line for line into the GLSL); no per-vertex tangents and no `Mesh` change. Normal maps are OpenGL (green up the image). The bump slider scales the normals (0.5 = as authored, 0 = flat, 1 = doubled); a height map with no normal map is turned into one (central difference, strength 6 x that scale). Roughness, metallic and ambient occlusion are packed into one data texture (R occlusion, G roughness, B metallic); occlusion darkens the ambient light in the GL view and the diffuse albedo in the ray tracer. An opacity map cuts the surface out below one half (a discard in GL, a pass-through ray in the tracer), not a blend. Maps beyond the albedo are shown for painted materials only (a mesh with its own colour), not for the stock scene materials. The GL view keeps maps to the Preferences > Render max texture size (default 2048), the ray tracer to 4096. | Planar tangent frame, cut-out opacity | 2026-10-08 |
| LB4 | Watch Downloads folder (Preferences > Render, off by default): polled every 3 s on the UI thread; a zip counts as "new" when it holds a `productmetadata.txt` and was modified within an hour before watching started or later. A half-written download fails to open and is looked at again as it grows. A `.zip` dropped anywhere on the window is treated as a material package (the Library Browser is not the only drop target; egui reports file drops for the whole window). | Window-wide drop, one-hour look-back | 2026-10-08 |
| NK1 | Span calculators (round 15, NKBA/structural): Header/Beam, Joist, Rafter and Deck spans in `plan-check/src/tables.rs` are computed from NDS reference design values (bending, shear, deflection, whole inches rounded down) rather than copied from the printed IRC R602.7, R502.3, R802.4 and DCA 6 tables, which are copyrighted; they are a design aid and the printed table of the adopted code decides. Southern Pine is not built in (its values depend on member width and NDS edition): pick Custom and type the supplement's values. | Computed spans; verify against the printed tables | 2026-10-08 |
| NK2 | NKBA rooms (round 15): a room is a kitchen when its Room Name type contains 'kitchen' and a bathroom when `Ctx::is_bath` says so; an unnamed room is not judged, so a plain plan raises no NKBA findings. Appliances and fixtures are found from placed library symbols (catalog id), countertop cutouts and cabinet appliances; distances between work centres are taken between the centres of their fronts. | Name the rooms Kitchen and Bath | 2026-10-08 |
| NK3 | NKBA severities (round 15): guidelines that are dimensions of safety or access are Warning (entry door, work triangle, landing areas, clearances, ventilation, GFCI); advisory guidelines are Info (two-cook aisle, waste receptacle, island corners, toilet centreline, shower control height, grab bar blocking, lavatory height, mirror). Guidelines the plan holds no data for (storage totals, lighting, exhaust cfm, traffic through the triangle, anti-scald valves) are listed as 'not checked' in the Kitchen and Bath report, never as failures. The whole group has one switch in Plan Check Settings (NKBA). | Group switch plus per-rule ticks | 2026-10-08 |
| NK4 | Kitchen and Bath report (round 15): Tools > Checks > Kitchen and Bath Report opens a text window (Markdown, one table per room) and Kitchen and Bath Report to Excel... writes the same rows (Room, Guideline, Requirement, Result, Detail) through plan-docs xlsx. Every guideline of every kitchen and bath is listed as Met, Not met, Not in plan, Not checked or Switched off. | Menu placement is ours; verify in Chief | 2026-10-08 |
| NK5 | Calculators (round 15): Tools > Calculators has Header/Beam, Joist Span, Rafter Span, Stair and Deck Beam/Joist in one window with a tab each. Apply writes one undo step: Header to the Framing tab of every selected door or window (sized from its own width), Joist and Rafter to the Framing Defaults (Build Framing uses them), Stair to the selected stair (rise, risers, tread), Deck to the selected deck room's Deck Specification. Chief has no such menu; the placement is ours. | Verify in Chief | 2026-10-08 |
| C15-1 | 360 panorama (round 15, C-77): 3D > Export > 360 Panorama... renders an equirectangular picture (2:1; 1024, 2048, 4096 or 8192 wide, clamped to the 24-megapixel limit) with the path tracer from the Full Camera's eye, the picture centre looking along the camera's heading and ignoring tilt; the PNG is saved where the dialog says and a single HTML page of the same name beside it (a JPEG data URI plus a small WebGL script, no libraries, a flat canvas fallback). A data URI rather than a link to the PNG because a WebGL texture from a file:// picture is blocked by browsers. Only a Full Camera or Floor Camera view can export (an overview has no eye); Chief's clickable virtual tour and cloud viewer are not built. Verify in Chief for the default size and the file types it writes. | Cube map faces; PNG only | 2026-10-08 |
| C15-2 | Walkthrough video (round 15, C-71): Record Walkthrough writes a Motion-JPEG AVI (`plan_render::AviWriter`, RIFF 'AVI ' with 'MJPG' frames and an idx1 index, baseline JPEG from `plan_render::encode_jpeg`, 4:4:4) or the PNG sequence plus make_video.sh, or both; the camera's `view.walk.format` (default Video) and `quality` (JPEG quality, default 85) are saved with it. The movie is `<camera name>.avi` in the chosen folder and is closed properly when the user cancels, so what was rendered plays. AVI 1.0 (no OpenDML), so a movie is limited to about 3.5 GB. Chief records through Windows Media/QuickTime; the container choice is ours. | MP4 through ffmpeg only; PNG sequence only | 2026-10-08 |
| C15-3 | Light sets (round 15, C-65): `Project.lighting.sets` holds named sets, each listing the ids of the plan lights and (apart, because their ids come from another counter) of the electrical fixtures that shine; `active_set` names the one the whole plan uses and `CameraView.light_set` a camera's own. With a set in use only the lights it lists shine (their own switch is ignored); with none each light follows its own switch. Made and edited in Adjust Lights (a new set starts from the lights that are on now), chosen in 3D > Lighting and on a camera's Rendering tab. A deleted light leaves every set. Chief's light sets may also store intensities; ours store on/off only (verify in Chief). `Lighting` is no longer `Copy`. | Store a copy of every light's settings | 2026-10-08 |
| C15-4 | Ground and fog (round 15, C-70): the Backdrop tab has Ground (default fade, a flat colour, or none so the sky carries on below the horizon) and Fog (on, distance in feet to about 63 percent fog, own colour or the horizon colour of the sky). Fog is exponential in the eye distance, applied in the scene shader to surfaces and the edge lines, not to the post-pass edge lines of Technical Illustration or Watercolor and not to a backdrop picture. Ground applies only in the looks that draw a sky. | Linear fog; fog in the post pass | 2026-10-08 |
| C15-5 | Techniques for pictures from the path tracer (round 15, C-45/47/49): the live view keeps its shader looks; recorded walkthroughs, panoramas and the path-traced Final View draw with the path tracer's Clay (Vector View, Line Drawing, Clay) or Physically Based shading and then `plan_render::stylize` (a Sobel edge detector on lightness plus flat shading tiers): Vector View is three grey tiers and black lines, Technical Illustration the material colour in four tiers with bold outlines, Line Drawing black lines on white, Watercolor a blur, paper grain and brown bleeding edges. The numbers are ours (verify in Chief). Glass House and Duotone have no path-tracer form and draw as Physically Based. | A GL post pass over the frame | 2026-10-08 |
| C15-6 | Undo Zoom in 3D (round 15, C-42): every run of frames in which the camera changes (a zoom, pan, orbit, move) is one step; 3D > Undo Zoom returns to the camera from before it (50 steps), including its mode. Programmatic changes (opening a view, framing, a playing walkthrough, Undo Zoom itself) are not steps, and opening another view clears the history. Chief steps back through zoom and pan only; ours includes orbits because the camera has no separate zoom state. The View > Undo Zoom item and its hotkey still run the plan's zoom (queue). | Zoom and pan only | 2026-10-08 |

| C15-9 | Path-traced Final View (round 15, C-53): Shading > Path-traced Final View (or 3D > View Quality) ray traces the view once the camera has been still for half a second and Final View quality is chosen, in a picture at most 960 wide refined in passes (64 samples, denoised at the end) and painted over the live view with a Stop button; moving the camera, selecting something, editing the plan, an orthographic view or Preview quality drop it. The sun is the plan's (3D > Lighting). Chief's Final View is a ray-traced refinement too, but the settle time and size are ours. Off by default. | Always on at Final quality | 2026-10-08 |

| CD1 | Polyline Union / Subtract / Intersect (round 15): Subtract keeps the first selected shape (selection order) and cuts the others out of it; Union of shapes that neither overlap nor touch, and Intersect of shapes that do not overlap, change nothing and say so; the result takes the layer, line style and fill of the first selected shape; a hole becomes a closed polyline of its own, grouped with its outline. Chief's exact buttons and results are unknown (verify in Chief). | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| CD2 | Drawing Groups (round 15): numbers 0 to 999; every kind starts in a group (Rooms 10, Foundation 15, Material Regions 20, Stairs 30, Cabinets and Symbols 40, Roofs 45, Walls 50, Doors and Windows 55, Electrical 60, Framing 65, Dimensions 70, CAD and Text 80, Schedules 85, Cameras 95); lower draws first. Bring to Front / Send to Back give the object one group above / below every other object of the floor; Set Drawing Group types a number. Only CAD objects and text re-order in the plan, Layout and sheet PDF (a CAD group below the Walls group draws under the walls); the other kinds keep their section order and store the number. Chief's numbers, kinds and direction are unknown (verify in Chief). Manual correction (p. 354): Chief puts CAD objects in Drawing Group 21, not 80 (the default for CAD and Text here is to become 21). | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| CD3 | Multiple Copy (round 15): the dialog takes the number of copies, the offset between copies (X, Y or distance and angle; or the offset to the last copy) and a turn per copy; "Drag in Plan" is two clicks (the point to copy from, then the position of the last copy) with a tick for every copy, the count being the dialog's last. The Alternate secondary direction is not built. Chief's exact interaction is unknown (verify in Chief). | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| CD4 | Trim to Boundary / Extend to Boundary (round 15): the boundary is clicked first (a CAD line, arc, circle, polyline or a wall's centerline), then any number of objects until Esc; Trim removes the clicked part up to the nearest crossings, a circle with two crossings becomes the arc left, Extend grows the end nearer the click to the first crossing along the line, arc or last segment. Chief's order of clicks is unknown (verify in Chief). | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| CD5 | DXF export options (round 15): the file stays R12 ASCII; line weights are written as group 370 in the LAYER table; text "as lines" uses a built-in single-stroke font (capitals, lowercase drawn as capitals); 3D means every floor at its elevation plus the 3D view's model as one 3DFACE per triangle on a layer named after the material. AutoCAD version choice, DWG and symbols as blocks are not built. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| CD6 | Plan Footprint (round 15): the union of the exterior walls' outlines (all walls when none is exterior), one closed polyline per detached outline, courtyards not part of it, with the area written under it. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| W15-1 | Wall drag chain (round 15, W-4): a press-drag-release draws one wall and the chain goes on from the release point (the next click ends a wall from there); Esc or a double click ends it. Chief's exact behavior is unverified (the other reading is that a drag draws a single wall and ends the chain): verify in Chief. | Chain continues | 2026-10-08 |
| W15-2 | Spacebar while drawing walls (round 15, W-22): reverses the layers of the walls drawn from then on (the exterior goes to the other side) and again flips it back; the ghost shows it. Chief's Spacebar use is unverified: verify in Chief. | Reverse layers | 2026-10-08 |
| W15-3 | Wall Covering (round 15, W-115): each face of a wall carries a full-height covering, a wainscot (material and height), chair rail, base and crown (profile names from the molding library; the height and projection come from the library) and a molding material. Bands break at doors and windows that reach into their height, stop at the joined corners and are built in 3D as slabs and swept profiles. Chief's field names, the stacking of two coverings and the cap/trim behavior at inside corners are unverified: verify in Chief. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| W15-4 | Newels/Balusters and Rails (round 15, W-116, W-117): defaults reproduce the railing built before the tabs (3 1/2 in square posts at most 96 in apart and at each end, 3/4 in square balusters 4 in apart, a top rail with its top at 36 in, a 1 1/2 in bottom rail 3 in off the floor). The baluster spacing is "greatest": the count is fixed by the bay, and a spacing wider than the 4 in sphere rule is allowed (plan check reads `WallRailing::widest_gap`). There is no separate handrail profile; the top rail is the handrail. Chief's wider Rail Style options (post to ceiling, step terrain, follow stairs) are not built. | Defaults match the old railing | 2026-10-08 |
| W15-5 | Generate Between Platforms (round 15, W-63): the invisible walls are regenerated (not edited) from the lower walls that ask for them and the upper walls that rest on them whenever a wall is connected or a Wall Specification is accepted; deleting a wall does not regenerate until the next of those (integration queue). The railing half ("and Railings") is not generated. Generated walls are never listed in the Wall schedule. | Regenerate on connect and OK | 2026-10-08 |
| W15-6 | Crossings with Split Walls at T-Intersections off (round 15, W-36): the overlap square is filled with the main-layer fill and one outline is drawn around the pair; the layer lines of each wall inside the overlap are covered rather than merged line by line. | Cover, then outline | 2026-10-08 |
| W15-7 | Wall schedule columns (round 15, W-118): the Wall schedule gains Wall Type, Interior Covering, Exterior Covering, Code, Description, Manufacturer, Model, Supplier and Comment (all hidden by default; Number, Type, Length, Thickness, Height, Area and Openings stay the default columns). A wall with Include in Schedule off, and the walls generated between platforms, are not numbered or listed. Chief's default Wall schedule columns are unverified: verify in Chief. | Hidden by default | 2026-10-08 |
| W15-8 | Wall Specification OK with nothing edited (round 15): leaves no undo step, so a dialog opened and closed does not appear in Edit > Undo. | No empty step | 2026-10-08 |
| W15-R1 | Wall Specification, Roof tab and Framing boxes (round 15): the Roof tab is live for every placed wall, Interior walls included (plan-3d `cover.rs` already drives a gable or knee interior wall from its directive), and for a multi-wall selection (the whole directive is one field, written to all walls once edited; unedited it shows a "(varies)" note). Retain Wall Framing is read from and written to the plan's framing settings (`retain_walls`), not the wall, in the dialog's own undo step. Bearing Wall is stored on the wall (`WallStructure.bearing_wall`, default off) but no framing rule reads it yet; Chief's default (on for exterior walls?) is unknown. Verify in Chief. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| DX1 | DWG import (round 15, L-78): not feasible without the Open Design Alliance's format spec, so a DWG is recognised by its `AC10xx` signature and the Import Drawing window says "This is an AutoCAD DWG file (release 2013). Plan Studio reads DXF: in AutoCAD, BricsCAD or LibreCAD choose Save As and pick DXF..." instead of reading it. Chief reads DWG through ODA; the wording is ours (verify in Chief). | Refuse with guidance | 2026-10-08 |
| DX2 | DXF reader home (round 15): the brief named `plan-core/src/dxf.rs`, but the reader lives in `plan-import` (plan-core has only the writer in `export/`). It was split into `plan-import/src/dxf.rs` (entry points) with `dxf/{tokens,read,model,geom,text,aci}.rs` and `convert.rs` with `convert/{flatten,dims,apply}.rs`; plan-import still has no crates.io dependency. Reads ASCII and binary DXF, R12 through 2018 (`$ACADVER` is kept, the group codes are the same family). | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| DX3 | Units when the file does not say (round 15): `$INSUNITS` 0 or absent reads as inches, or millimetres when `$MEASUREMENT` is 1 (Chief's "inches or millimeters"). `$INSUNITS` 21 (US survey foot) reads as feet. The Drawing Unit page can override, and Scale multiplies on top. `$DIMSCALE` and the DIMSTYLE sizes are used only to size dimension arrows and text of dimensions that have to be drawn from their definition points. | Inches / mm by $MEASUREMENT | 2026-10-08 |
| DX4 | CAD blocks from INSERTs (round 15): each top-level INSERT (and MINSERT array) with more than one resulting object becomes one CAD block named after the block definition, its insertion point the INSERT's. Plan Studio's CAD blocks are flat groups, so nested INSERTs are expanded into the outer block. Block definitions no INSERT uses are not placed (there is no block library to keep them; the last page says how many). Duplicate names against the floor: Auto Name (`name_Copy_1`), Replace (the floor's blocks of that name are deleted) or Use Existing (a copy of the floor's block goes at each INSERT's insertion point, without its scale or rotation, because blocks are copies and not shared definitions). Create CAD Blocks makes the whole drawing one block instead. Chief's exact rules are unknown (verify in Chief). | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| DX5 | HATCH (round 15): the boundary loops come in as closed polylines (arc and spline edges sampled); a SOLID hatch is a solid fill on the outer loop; a pattern hatch is drawn with the CAD Hatch tool's lines on the outer loop, choosing the tool's pattern by the AutoCAD pattern name (ANSI31 to Diagonal Lines, ANSI37 and NET to Cross Hatch, BRICK to Brick, AR-CONC to Concrete, EARTH to Earth, INSUL to Insulation; unknown names by the pattern angle) with the spacing taken from the pattern definition. The pattern angle is not honoured (the tool's patterns have fixed angles), islands inside the outer loop get outlines but no fill, and gradient hatches are solid. Chief imports hatches as solid black polylines; ours keeps the look (verify in Chief). | Nearest tool pattern | 2026-10-08 |
| DX6 | DIMENSION (round 15, L-79): linear and aligned dimensions become plan dimensions (a rotated one measures along its direction, so its end is projected onto it; a text override is kept, `<>` replaced by the measurement). plan-core has no angular, radius, diameter or ordinate dimension, so those are drawn as lines, arc, arrowheads and text, from the DXF's own anonymous `*D` block when it is in the file and from the definition points and the dimension style otherwise. "Import as CAD blocks" draws every dimension that way, each in a CAD block named Dimension. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| DX7 | Text from DXF (round 15): TEXT and MTEXT are anchored by their justification (the plan's text position is the lower left of its block, estimated with the plan's glyph width factor); MTEXT formatting (`\f` font and bold/italic, `\H`, `\C`, `\L`, `\S` stacks, `\P`) becomes rich runs, the first font names the whole text, a reference width becomes the text box width and the attachment its alignment; a DXF text style whose name matches a plan text style is kept. ATTRIB values become texts at their place and replace the block's ATTDEFs (invisible ones are dropped); an ATTDEF with no ATTRIB shows its default value. LEADER and MULTILEADER become a polyline with a filled arrow at the first point plus the MULTILEADER's text. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| DX8 | Smaller entities (round 15): a POINT becomes a circle of 1 in diameter (Chief ignores points unless a layer becomes Elevation Data, which is not offered here); SOLID and TRACE are filled outlines, 3DFACE and polyface meshes outlines, all with Z dropped; 3D meshes and every other kind (IMAGE, REGION, ACIS solids, VIEWPORT, XLINE...) are skipped and counted on the last page. An entity with an extrusion pointing down (0,0,-1) is mirrored in x like AutoCAD does. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| DX9 | Colours, weights and line types (round 15): the ACI table is computed (index 7 prints black), true colours (group 420) are kept. With "layers of the same names" the new plan layers take the DXF layer's colour, weight and line style and only objects that differ carry their own; with a single layer, or without the layer attributes, every object carries its colour, weight and line style (the manual: "line color, style, and weight are preserved on a per object basis"). plan-core has no custom line styles, so a line type becomes the nearest of solid, dashed, dotted and dash-dot, by name (HIDDEN, CENTER, DOT...) and then by its dash pattern. BYBLOCK and layer 0 follow the INSERT. | Nearest built-in style | 2026-10-08 |
| DX10 | Paper space and xrefs (round 15): objects in the first page of paper space are skipped unless "Include the first page of paper space" is on, and then come in as one CAD block named Paper Space; later pages are never read. External references are listed as missing files (their blocks are empty) on the Select File page and the last page. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| DX11 | Convert to walls in the assistant (round 15): a layer marked To walls has its lines and polyline segments run through CAD to Walls with the default options in the same undo step, the lines staying as CAD (like the CAD to Walls tool). Picking a wall type makes the walls take that type's total thickness, kind and name; "Default for its kind" keeps the measured thickness. Chief's Convert To list (Terrain Perimeter, Elevation Data) is not built. | Lines stay | 2026-10-08 |
| DX12 | Not built from the Import Drawing dialog (round 15): Place In Current View off and Add to Library (there is no way to put CAD blocks into the User Catalog from here), import into sections and layouts, passwords. A .dxf or .dwg dropped on the window opens the same window. Auto Position Blocks puts several files side by side (48 in apart) instead of stacking them at the origin. | Listed as open | 2026-10-08 |
| CL1 | Callout/Marker/Note model (round 15, TXT-49..56): a callout, marker or note is a record in `Floor::annots` (`plan_core::callout`) plus the plain, grouped CAD objects it generates, so it draws, prints (layout, PDF, DXF), selects, moves and deletes like any CAD object. `Project::sync_annotations` (run by the editor refresh) keeps the objects equal to the record, takes a move or turn of the group back into the record first, drops records whose objects were deleted, and expands the annotation macros. Pasting or copying a callout makes plain CAD (the clipboard does not clone the record yet); verify in Chief. | Record plus generated CAD | 2026-10-08 |
| CL2 | The ten callout shapes (manual p. 563 shows them as pictures only): Circle, Oval, Square, Rectangle, Rounded Rectangle, Hexagon, Octagon, Triangle, Half Circle, Diamond, plus None. Chief's exact set and order is unverified; verify in Chief. | These ten | 2026-10-08 |
| CL3 | Callout text sizes use the plain character height of the record (plan inches), not a printed-size style's height at the sheet scale, so a Printed Size style does not change the shape size; the label still draws in the style's font. | Plan height | 2026-10-08 |
| CL4 | Notes: "Generate Shape/Fill/Size/Angles from Schedule" resolve to the Callout defaults (circle, no fill, automatic size, angle 0) because Plan Studio's Note Schedule has no Labels settings (L-73); they default off so the Shape buttons work. A Note Schedule lists a Note Type when its `filter` is empty or is part of the type's name (Create Note Schedule from Note(s) sets it); the Caution symbol shows until such a schedule exists. Notes drawn in the old text style ("Note 3: ...") still list. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| CL5 | The Link View dialog is a menu on the Link panel (camera views, CAD details, layout pages); the layout page label is `A-<number>` of the first page that holds a box of the camera or detail (`plan_core::callout::PageInfo`, read from the layout JSON because plan-core cannot see plan-layout). Find in Layout reports the page in the status line; it does not switch windows. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| CL6 | Rich Text Edit Bar: every format is a markup tag on the selection (`<font=>`, `<size=>`, `<color=>`, `<b> <i> <u> <s> <upper>`, `<link=>`); bullets and numbering are text prefixes on the lines (not a paragraph model), alignment is the whole box's. Insert Hyperlink makes a blue underlined run that remembers its address; Follow Hyperlink opens it with the system opener. The in-plan strip applies the same to the whole text when it is placed. Spacing, margins and indents of Paragraph Options are not built. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| ML1 | Materials List data (round 15, L-84..L-137): saved Live Lists and Reports, Materials List Polylines and the information objects carry live in `Project::materials` (`plan_core::materials_data`, `#[serde(default)]`, left out of the file while empty, so older plans load). Objects are addressed by the Property Manager's keys (`wall:12`, `door:5`, `device:0:4`, `room:0:120,84`) plus `foundation:<id>`; a typed cell of a line that stands for no object is filed under `line:<master key>`. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| ML2 | A Materials List Polyline is a closed CAD polyline of the floor on the layer "Materials List Polylines" plus a record (`MaterialsPolyline`: the Included Floors/Categories grid and the Included Objects choice). It moves, stretches and takes holes as a CAD polyline does; deleting the polyline leaves a record the lists report as "gone". Chief's Convert Polyline into a Materials List Polyline and the polyline's own Line/Fill Style panels are not built (use the CAD Specification); verify in Chief. | CAD polyline plus record | 2026-10-08 |
| ML3 | The Categories panel only hides categories in the window and the printout; hidden categories stay in the list, in its totals and in every export, as the manual says (p. 1373). The exported file always carries all categories. Verify in Chief. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| ML4 | Objects that carry different information (a supplier, a price, a code, a removed or recounted component) get rows of their own; objects that agree stay one row, and the row remembers every object behind it (`Source`). Information typed on the object applies to all of its lines; a Components row's own Price, Markup, Labor, Equipment or Extra wins over the object's. A typed cell of a live list is written to every object behind the row. | Rows split by information | 2026-10-08 |
| ML5 | Columns: Count is the net count plus the Master List's waste, rounded up (waste is our addition; Chief's Count is the actual number), Extra is added to it, and Total Cost is the manual's formula. Use, Quantity and Default exist only in the Master List. The older eight-column `to_csv`/`row_cells`/xlsx writers (layout boxes, construction set) are unchanged. | As described, waste kept | 2026-10-08 |
| ML6 | Master List matching follows the manual: same Category, Size, Description and Label (or the same Code); an entry the user marked Default wins, else the last one entered; an entry with a Quantity applies only from that count; an entry whose Use box is clear removes its rows. Entries from older files (key, unit, unit price, supplier) still price by key. What an object carries wins over the Master List, field by field. Live lists apply the Master List on every calculation; Update From Master List is for Reports. | Chief's rules | 2026-10-08 |
| ML7 | Calculate Materials in Room: the room's contents by object center, its floor and ceiling finish rows, the wall surface facing it (each edge of the inner outline times the height of the wall it runs along, less the openings in that stretch, as Wall drywall sheets) and the doors and windows set in its walls (center inside, or within half a wall thickness plus 2" of the outline). The surrounding walls' framing and siding are not counted. A selected room alone gives its finishes, not doors, windows or framing. Moldings, subfloor and wall-type layers of the facing walls are not counted (the take-off has no molding or subfloor rows); verify in Chief. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| ML8 | Structural Member Reporting is a per-list choice: Buy List (stock lengths, spliced past the longest), Cut List (one row per size and cut length) or Linear Feet (per size); board feet are always reported. The toolbar drop-down and saved reporting defaults are not built. Auto framing follows its wall in Selection, Room and Area lists; manual members follow their own position. | Per list | 2026-10-08 |
| ML9 | Export: XML is Excel 2003 Spreadsheet XML (numbers and currency typed); HTML honors the list's grid and colors; units go with the amount, in a new column or nowhere; hidden categories are exported (ML3). BuilderTREND's CSV uses Cost Code (Accounting Code), Title, Description, Quantity, Unit Cost, Unit and Markup %: the column set is a best guess at its import, verify against BuilderTREND. | Best guess for BuilderTREND | 2026-10-08 |
| ML10 | Print writes the visible columns as a paged PDF table (landscape when the columns are wide) through the existing print delivery (printer, viewer, PDF file). The File > Print route for an active Materials List window is not hooked (queued for the print owner). | Window button and menu | 2026-10-08 |
| ML11 | Components and Object Information are two extra tabs the shared specification frame adds after Properties (`dialogs::object_info`, hosted like the Properties session by `spec_dialogs`, `main.rs` for walls and openings, and the room dialog). A dialog that already has a tab of that name gets "Components (Materials List)" / "Object Information (Materials List)". OK applies them with the dialog as one undo step. Formulas (the Formula column of the Materials List Data table) are not built. | Extra tabs | 2026-10-08 |
| I2B-1 | Plan overlay (integration pass 2): cabinets (fill, symbol, label), merged countertops (dashed), placed symbols, stairs and the lower floor's stairs (break-line part and, through a stairwell, dashed treads) reach plan boxes, Print Preview, the PDF and the floor DXF through one neutral list (`plan_layout::PlanOverlayItem`, built by `editor/plan_overlay.rs`). A cabinet's Fill Style prints in the layer's colour (hatch) or as the fill colour at its transparency over white (solid); the DXF has no dashes here, so dashed items go on `"<layer>, Hidden"` ("Stairs, Hidden") and solid fills are left out. Verify in Chief. |
| I2B-2 | File > New Layout when the plan has a layout: it opens the New Layout File name dialog (a second file; the open layout is parked in `Project::layout_files`) instead of "opens the existing one". Verify in Chief. |
| I2B-3 | Page drawings in a group selection: Shift-click adds a page drawing (CAD, leader, cloud) to the selection beside the selected boxes; dragging any of them moves all (one undo step). Boxes and drawings are otherwise selected apart. |
| I2B-4 | Auto Place Outlets, kitchen counter outlets: they stand only on wall spaces a run of base-type cabinets (or anything with a countertop) is within 6" of; a kitchen with no cabinet on its walls keeps outlets on every wall as before. |
| ML12 | The old window's By Surface tab (dialogs/materials.rs, the Materials tools' builder) is kept as a separate window opened by the By Surface button; the new window replaces the old List and Master tabs. Closing a list that is new or changed asks to save it, as Chief does. | Kept | 2026-10-08 |
| CL1 | Construction lines (round 16, CAD-62..CAD-67) are CAD lines (`CadItem::Line`, so they select, move, copy and delete as CAD objects) plus a `ConstructionLine` record in `Floor::construction` keyed by the CAD id (`plan_core::construction`, left out of the file while empty). A record without its CAD line is ignored and pruned. A copy through the clipboard is a plain CAD line (the record is not cloned yet). | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| CL2 | New construction lines start infinite in plan and elevation, on one floor only, in automatic ordering, with no callouts (Callouts: None). The manual does not give Chief's starting values; Construction Line Defaults and Set as Default change them. Verify in Chief. | Callouts None | 2026-10-08 |
| CL3 | The "Construction Lines" layer is added when the first line is drawn: blue, dashed, 0.13 mm. The manual names the layer but not its look. Drawing Group 21 (manual p. 84) is set per object (an override on the line), because the drawing group table's fifteen kinds are not construction-line aware. Verify in Chief. | Blue dashed | 2026-10-08 |
| CL4 | Order Management starts with four system rule sets: Plan Vertical (90 degrees, 1, 2, 3 from left to right), Plan Horizontal (0 degrees, A, B, C from the top down), and the same two for Elevation View. Angles match within half a degree in either direction; lines within a quarter inch of one another are collinear and share a number. Chief's own system rule sets are not documented. Verify in Chief. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| CL5 | Manual contradiction: page 83 of the reference manual starts with a stray Room Box "General panel" paragraph (Build House, room name, function) before the Construction Lines text; it belongs to the Room Box and is ignored here. The manual says infinite lines are "not included in the view extents"; Fill Window and layout extents still count the drawn stub (queued in docs/integration-queue.md). | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| CL6 | Construction lines snap: endpoints, midpoints and crossings of the drawn stubs through the CAD snaps; the extension of an infinite line (its crossing with another infinite line or a reference wall, and the nearest point on it, as On Object) through very long reference segments in `snap_with_reference` (any segment over 1,000,000 inches is an infinite line). A dimension, wall or CAD point snapped there measures from it. Associative dimensions that follow a construction line are not built. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| RD1 | The Change Floor/Reference table is kept in `Project::reference_table` (rows front to back, where the Current line sits, XOR), left out of the file while it is the default. While it is empty one row stands for the session's Reference Display choices as before (the floor below, gray). With a table the first row of this plan is what `reference_display::settings` returns. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| RD2 | Reference rows draw walls only (as the single row did): Details on fills them dimmed, off draws edge lines only. XOR drawing drops a reference edge that matches an edge of the current floor's walls (within half an inch) and draws the ones laid over the walls in the row color's complement; Chief's exact recoloring is not documented. Verify in Chief. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| RD3 | Another plan file is read as a Plan Studio file (`.psplan`) at run time, read only, and cached until its file time changes. Its X/Y offset and Angle map its points into the current plan (turned about its own origin, then moved); the Z offset is stored and not used in plan. Edit Reference Document Offset turns about the marquee's center so that point stays put. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| RD4 | Go to Reference Floor (Swap Floor/Reference) is not an undo step: it changes the floor shown and which floor is the reference, like Up One Floor. With the default single row it keeps the choice in the session settings, with a table it edits the first row. It has a toolbar button and a menu row and no default hotkey. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| RD5 | Tools > Floor/Reference Display is now a submenu (Change Floor/Reference, Swap Floor/Reference, Edit Reference Document Offset) and the Reference Display Options button opens the new dialog through `Action::Custom(reference_display::CHANGE)`. The older `Action::ReferenceDisplayOptions` and its `FloorDialog::Reference` path still work but only keep the first row. The dialogs of this round are shown every frame by `ToolSet::frame`, so they open whichever tool is active. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| PW1 | Customize Sheet Sizes is program-wide (round 16, L-8; supersedes row 63, Daniel's standing instruction to match Chief, manual p. 1430: one file in the program's Data folder). The sizes live in `~/.plan-studio/sheetsizes.json` (`plan_layout::SheetSizeFile`: custom sizes and the hidden standard sizes). A layout's own `custom_sizes` still load and are taken over by name when its Print dialog or its sheet list is built (`adopt_layout_sheet_sizes`); an old layout's hidden list is not taken over (it was that layout's choice, not the program's). File > Print > Customize Sheet Sizes, the Layout menu entry and the layout toolbar button all open the one program-wide dialog (`main.rs` turns `LayoutCommand::CustomizeSheetSizes` into it). The layout's Page Setup list still offers the per-layout list until `layout_window` reads `global_sheet_sizes()` (integration queue). Verify in Chief that the list is also shared with the layout's Page Setup. | Program-wide file | 2026-10-08 |
| PW2 | Drawing Sheet Setup per view (round 16, L-199, L-200, L-204, L-205, L-207): the plan keeps a table, `Project::print_setup`, with one setup for each kind of view (plan, cross section / elevation, CAD Detail, layout, Materials List). A kind with no entry of its own uses the plan's (manual p. 1426: a new view takes the plan view's settings); a layout with none is 1 in = 1 in (the manual says 1 in = 1 in on p. 1422 and 1 ft = 1 ft on p. 1428: both are a ratio of 1). A layout's Drawing Sheet Setup is its Page Setup, so File > Print > Drawing Sheet Setup opens that while the layout shows. The app has no print target for an elevation or Materials List view yet, so the dialog has a View drop-down to reach their setups. The editor's sheet (`EditorContext::sheet`) follows a plan setup only when the plan stores one and it changed, so a fresh session and the Print Preview override keep working as before. | Per view type in the plan | 2026-10-08 |
| PW3 | Print dialog memory (round 16, L-201, L-219, L-223): settings are kept per kind of view in `~/.plan-studio/printsettings.json` for every plan when the view's setup has Remember Print Settings after Printing on; copies, the page range and Print Source are never kept (Print Source follows the Drawing Sheet toggle); Fit to Paper is 95% of the paper and global. The first print of a kind starts from its Drawing Sheet Setup: Match Print Source paper, To Scale when the sheet is shown, else Fit to Paper. Include Watermark starts ticked when View > Watermark is on in the view (the manual does not say; verify). Unchecking Remember uses the setup's printer instead. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| PW4 | Check Plot (round 16, L-211): the view is drawn at its true scale and the whole sheet is printed at the chosen fraction, so line weights shrink with it (manual p. 1433); the list offers 3/4, 2/3, 1/2, 1/3, 1/4 and 1/8. The paper adjusts to the smallest listed paper that holds the reduced sheet plus the printing margins (so 18 x 12 with 1/4 inch margins is ARCH C, not ARCH B), in the sheet's orientation. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| PW5 | Drawing Margins and Populate from Printer (round 16, L-207, L-221): the Drawing Margins of a plan view are also the printing margins of the paper when the paper matches the sheet (the printable area is the sheet inside them, the blue border on screen). CUPS gives no portable imageable area, so Populate from Printer puts 1/4 inch on every side when there is a printer (or a default one) and 0 for none; verify against a real printer. Paper Source sends the CUPS `InputSlot` (`-o InputSlot=Tray1`), Collate sends `-o collate=true`, and Open System Print Dialog opens the PDF in the viewer, whose Print command is the system dialog. DPI only sizes Print Image pictures and is told in the messages: vector prints do not depend on it. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| PW6 | Watermark (round 16, L-53, L-214..L-217): the mark and the views it is on in are file-specific (`print_setup.watermark`); the toggle is keyed by the saved plan view's name (`plan:<name>`), `detail`, `elevation` and `layout` (one switch for every page), so renaming a saved view drops its switch (move it to `SavedPlanView` when the saved-views owner agrees). Print Size is the height of a capital A (0.718 of the font size). Tile is Marks per Row by Marks per Column cells inside the margins, a mark centred in each; Border runs the first and last rows along the top and bottom and the middle rows down the sides, corners counted once; Fit to Sheet scales one turned mark to fill the area (Print Size and Ratio to Sheet unused). A picture is Ratio to Sheet of the area's width, turned about its centre; its transparency is a PDF soft mask. Text is one line (line breaks become spaces) and plain: no page macros. The Chief defaults are not documented; Plan Studio starts at DRAFT, grey, 0.5 inch, 30 degrees, 70% transparent, 3 x 3 tiles, Drawing Sheet margins. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| PW7 | Line weights (round 16, L-208, L-212, L-213): a view's Line Weight Scale multiplies every pen made for 1 = 1/100 mm; "Use 1 for all line weights" prints every line 1/300 inch (0.24 pt), unless Print line weights is off, which stays the 0.5 pt hairline; exact weights take the print scale out of every pen so a sheet printed smaller keeps its thickness (not for a Check Plot, which shrinks lines on purpose). Plot Lines on a layout box and the Layout Line Scaling switch need a field on `LayoutBox` (layout boxes owner) and are not built. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| PW8 | Drawing Sheet on screen (round 16, L-209, L-210): the sheet's border moves it, its corner handles resize it (a resize makes a custom size named by its sides), Center Sheet and Scale to Fit put it at the middle of the floor's walls and dimensions, all stored per floor in `Floor::sheet_center`; none moves a coordinate. Each is one undo step. Scale to Fit picks the largest architectural scale (then 1:250, 1:500, 1:1000, 1:2000) that fits the plan inside the sheet's Drawing Margins and keeps a metric scale metric. Files outside the brief's list that had to change for this: `editor/sheet.rs` (`SheetSetup::paper_cin` for custom and upright sheets, `plan_center` reads `sheet_center`), `dialogs.rs`, `scenarios/mod.rs` (mod lines), `main.rs` and `menus.rs` (shared). | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| ST15-1 | Ramp tool (round 15, CB-34): one click draws a 1:12 ramp 30 in high and 360 in long (manual p. 776); a drag sets the run and the slope follows (30 in of rise per run, a run steeper than 1:12 is drawn and flagged in the status bar, the dialog and Plan Check "IRC R311.8 ramps"); a rise over 30 in is split into runs of 30 in with 60 in landings. `plan_check::CodeMinimums` has no ramp field, so the 1:12 limit is the IBC 1012.2 constant `plan_stairs::RAMP_MIN_SLOPE`. Ramps get a handrail on both sides by default. Verify in Chief. | As described | 2026-10-08 |
| ST15-2 | Downward stairs (round 15, CB-106, CB-109, CB-110): Alt held while drawing, or a right-button drag, draws the stair downward from the press (the top, at the floor) to the release (the bottom); the arrow reads DN. The manual (p. 778) says Alt reverses the Up arrow and (p. 779) that porch-to-terrain stairs are drawn downward with Alt or the right button; one gesture serves both. Its rise is the drop to the terrain under the bottom when the plan has terrain there, else 30 in. Snaps are suppressed by Ctrl/Cmd. Verify in Chief. | As described | 2026-10-08 |

A click on or within 20 ft of a deck (or any room) picks the nearest edge; the stair is centred on the click along the edge, leaves outward, starts at the room's finished floor and ends on the terrain (the deck's height above grade when there is no terrain). Over 30 in of rise both sides get a guard, else a wall handrail. | As described | 2026-10-08 |

| BS1 | Architectural blocks (round 15, CB-427..CB-438): a block is a record in `Floor::blocks` (`plan_core::arch_block`) that lists its members by `ObjectRef`; the members stay in their own stores (cabinets, symbols, devices, slabs, 3D solids, molding polylines, compound solids, other blocks). A click on any member selects every member of the outermost block (`selection::expand_groups`), so a block moves, turns, mirrors, copies and deletes as one; Edit > Edit Sub-Objects (Edit toolbar button) turns that off until it is switched off again. The plan box is the box around the members, drawn dash-dot with the label on "Architectural Blocks, Labels". Not built: roads, terrain features and modifiers as members (they are not addressable objects), Add to Library As for blocks, 3D regeneration on move, Select Next Object into a block, hiding the sub-objects when Display Sub-Objects is off (only the box and label are drawn in addition). The operands are consumed (one undo step brings them back).
