### Round 15 robustness (QA-20 to QA-29): foreign keys by comparison, not `#[serde(flatten)]`
Unknown keys are kept by comparing the loaded JSON with what the typed plan serializes to (`plan-core/src/foreign.rs`, `Foreign::capture`/`apply`) and writing the difference back at the same place on save (objects are found again by id or name). This covers every depth (walls, openings, rooms, cabinets...) with one mechanism and no `extra` map on each struct, and it does not change the serialized shape of any struct. Cost: a load serializes the plan once more. NaN and infinity are written as 0 (`Finite` serializer wrapper, `Project::sanitize`) and the status bar says so on save, instead of refusing to save. Unreadable records in untyped lists (electrical devices, cabinets, roofs) are kept as raw JSON and written back after the edited ones.

### Round 16: tray ceilings (brief 16)
| # | Decision | Assumption in code until decided | Raised |
|---|---|---|---|
| TC1 | A tray ceiling is a closed CAD polyline on the "Ceiling Planes" layer plus a `TrayCeiling` record in `Floor::trays` keyed by the polyline id (`plan_core::tray`), the way construction lines are, so selection, move, stretch, copy and delete come from the CAD object. Chief's layer for the polyline is not in the manual; "Ceiling Planes" is used because the tray is made of ceiling planes. | The layer name and the CAD-polyline storage; verify in Chief. | 2026-10-09 |
| TC2 | The polyline is the edge of the hole (the manual's Polyline panel reports the "interior" perimeter and area). The outer ceiling is the ring of `width` inches around it, derived from the polyline (offset outward), so moving the polyline moves the ring. Manual p. 458 says Width is only set by the Make dialogs; it is kept in the record so the ring survives. | Width default 24 in, Depth default 8 in, Make Nested defaults 12 in and 4 in (Chief's defaults are not published; Tray Ceiling Defaults, RF-65, will replace them). | 2026-10-09 |
| TC3 | Manual contradiction: p. 457 says a tray is "an upper and lower Ceiling Plane and a ceiling plane hole", p. 459 says without Recess the inner ceiling sits at the room's ceiling height and the outer ceiling drops. Built: the inner ceiling is the room's own ceiling platform (no plane) unless Recess into Ceiling is on, when it is a raised plane and the platform gets a hole; Explode Tray Ceiling follows the same rule. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| TC4 | Sloped sides: the sloped plane lies in the outer ring along the hole edge with horizontal run `depth * 12 / pitch`; a run wider than the Width is a Caution (SlopeTooWide). Pitch zero or less is Vertical (the dialog's -89 to 89 degree range is accepted but a flat or inverted side has no step). Moldings and Rope Lights are off while sloped (p. 459). | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| TC5 | Caution (no 3D, no framing, no Explode, symbol in plan): not a usable shape, not inside one room, room without a flat ceiling (Flat Ceiling Over This Room off or no ceiling), no depth, slope wider than the ring. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| TC6 | Nesting is by containment: the smallest tray whose hole holds this tray's hole is its parent and its base height is the parent's inner ceiling; coffers are trays side by side in one ceiling. Make Coffered Ceiling in Room (not a Chief button; Chief copies nested trays with Multiple Copy) makes recessed trays of about 36 in with 8 in beams, 6 in deep, a ring of half a beam. | Defaults as described. | 2026-10-09 |
| TC7 | Flat Ceiling Over This Room is `RoomName.flat_ceiling` (default true, serde default). `plan_core::tray::room_flat` is flag and "Ceiling Over This Room" together. A cathedral room has no flat plate; its ceiling planes are made at scene build from the floor's roof planes (`plan_3d::tray::cathedral_planes`) unless Build Ceiling Planes already put a ceiling plane over the room (the older `has_ceiling` off route of `roof_view::vaulted_ceilings` is untouched). | As described; brief 15 owns the checkbox. | 2026-10-09 |
| TC8 | Shelf (plant shelf) ceiling: a room nested inside a cathedral room (a hole in the room, R-11) with its own ceiling height is cut out of the cathedral planes (convex pieces, exact) and keeps its flat ceiling at its own height. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| TC9 | Rope lights are runs along the hole outline moved `h_offset` away from the hole at the lower ceiling plus `v_offset` (defaults 1 in, 2 in); `plan_core::tray::rope_light_paths` hands them to the electrical layer. Moldings hang from the upper ceiling by their height on the hole outline (`molding_runs`). | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| TC10 | Framing (`plan_framing::frame_tray_ceiling`): a wall of plates and studs just outside the hole edge for vertical sides (2x stud size and spacing of the Framing Defaults), rafters up the slope for sloped sides, ceiling joists at the tray's Rafter Spacing over the dropped ring (with the hole as a stairwell-style hole) and over a raised inner ceiling. Retain Framing and any Caution make no members. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| TC11 | Explode Tray Ceiling: ceiling plane records for the outer ceiling pieces, the raised inner ceiling (Recess) and one plane per sloped side; the polyline stays as a plain CAD polyline; each molding and rope light run becomes a closed polyline on "Moldings" / "Rope Lights". One undo step. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| TC12 | The Materials List line for the step (`plan_core::tray::step_lines`): side area, outer ceiling area, molding and rope light lengths, Components. Not yet called by the take-off (plan-docs is another owner's); see docs/integration-queue.md. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| CR1 | Automatic fillers (CB-480): the fillers are stored cabinets (`Cabinet::auto_filler`, kind Base/Wall/FullHeight Filler) rebuilt by `tools::cabinet::sync_auto_fillers` after every cabinet edit (called from `placed::rejoin_if_enabled`, so one undo step with the edit), not computed at draw time, so 3D, the Materials List, merging and the generated countertop see them like any cabinet. They are not picked (`hit_cabinet`), not in the Cabinet Schedule (`in_schedule` false), have a blank label, and never block, bump or push (the tools read the floor without them). A filler that is still right keeps its id and is not rewritten. Chief generates them inside the cabinets; the stored form is the one that fits this model. | As described; verify in Chief how an edit near a filler behaves. | 2026-10-09 |
| CR2 | Filler rules (manual p. 649): between two same-family, same-height cabinets standing side by side, and between a cabinet side and a wall, with 1/16 in to 3 in of gap, the filler is exactly the gap (rounded to 1/16 in), as deep as the two have in common, with the first cabinet's height, toe kick, countertop, backsplash and moldings. Extended stiles (`stile_ext_left/right`) count towards the gap. Base next to full height, or wall next to base, get none (different heights). Corner units take no fillers on their own arms. Touching cabinets need none. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| CR3 | Angled connections (manual pp. 649, 651; the picture is not in the text): where two same-family cabinets of different angles face each other and one's front corner lies 1/16 to 3 in from the other's front corner, along the first one's end, the filler goes beside that end of the first (next to its end face, as deep as it), so the corner stays open and the fronts clear each other. Back corners (facing away, at most 87 degrees between the fronts) merge but get no filler. The reading of 'angle' as the angle between the fronts is a guess. | As described; verify in Chief. | 2026-10-09 |
| CR4 | Cabinet merging (CB-482): `plan_cabinets::merge_runs(cabs, reach)` groups cabinets of one family and height that stand side by side within the reach (3 in with Create Automatic Fillers, touching only without it), or meet at a front corner, or at a back corner facing away at no more than 87 degrees. Shared toe kick, countertop, backsplash and moldings stay per cabinet; the generated countertop and the new module lines read the runs. `merge_runs` is the hook for brief 24's display merge of countertops (DECISIONS 18). | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| CR5 | Module lines (CB-634): the lines where merged cabinets meet are drawn dashed on the new layer 'Cabinets, Module Lines' (added to the plan with the first filler sync); the shared end faces of the cabinets are left out of their outlines, so turning the layer off shows one block. Show Partial Module Lines draws a short grey tick in the middle of the join instead. Extended stiles add their small frame lines on the same layer. The tick length (a third of the shared depth, at most 4 in) is a guess. | As described; verify in Chief. | 2026-10-09 |
| CR6 | Snap reach (manual p. 650; changes DECISIONS 53): a resized edge dragged within 3 in of a wall or the next cabinet snaps to it (was 4 in); a back or front within 3 in of a neighbour's lines up (was 6 in); back-to-back and perpendicular snapping reach is 3 in (was 6 in). A cabinet dropped within 3 in of another is not pulled to touch it: the gap gets an automatic filler instead (Ctrl and the dimensions override both, as in the manual). | As described; verify in Chief. | 2026-10-09 |
| CR7 | Automatic label (CB-488, manual pp. 655 to 657): Key B/W/U, Code (SB, RB, OB, #DB, FHB, DC/LC/LS/LSD/BC before the key, P/F/XL/XR/XLR after it, OTC, RTC), Size, door swing L or R only when all doors swing one way (an Auto door gives no letter). The manual's size table swaps the Depth and Height rows of the wall block; its examples (BCW2436R: width, height, depth omitted when standard) win: a wall cabinet always shows its height, depth only when not 12 in (corners: when not the width). Library types (Vanity, Pantry, Tall Oven, Refrigerator) keep their own letters (VB, PN, OC, REF) and the `<T>` macro keeps the old type codes. The old forms B36-SB, FH2484, FHF3, BBC48, WBC, BDC, BLS, BPC, RNG24 and CT are gone; custom countertops now have a blank label as the manual says. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| CR8 | Blank labels: shelves, partitions, custom countertops, backsplashes, counter holes and automatic fillers have a blank automatic label; manual fillers read BF3, WF330, UF32484 (the manual lists Key+F as a code, so manual fillers are not blank, unlike the brief's wording). Suppress Label (`Cabinet::suppress_label`) hides a label; the Label tab check box is queued for the dialog's owner (docs/integration-queue.md). | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| CR9 | General Cabinet Defaults (CB-476, manual pp. 644 and 645): `PlanDefaults::cabinets.general` (`GeneralCabinetDefaults`), edited by the new `dialogs/cabinet_defaults.rs` from Default Settings > Cabinets > General Cabinet (nowhere else). Starting values keep the old behaviour: Minimum Cabinet Width 3 in, Resize Increment 3 in (Use Resize Increment), fillers on; Minimum Shelf Spacing 6 in and Auto Door Threshold 24 in (the tutorial's one door under 24 in) are stored but nothing reads them yet (queue). Create Automatic Fillers is dynamic: OK rebuilds the fillers in one undo step. Display Molding Edges sits here because the brief says so; the manual puts it in the Base Cabinet and Custom Countertop specifications. | As described; verify in Chief. | 2026-10-09 |
| CR10 | Minimum cabinet size (manual p. 651): a base, wall or full height cabinet clicked into a space bounded by walls or cabinets on both sides that is narrower than its width takes the largest multiple of the Resize Increment that fits (24 in in 20 in with 3 in: 18 in), never below the Minimum Cabinet Width; in a space narrower than the minimum nothing is placed and the status line says so. The space is read before bumping, because bumping slides a cabinet out of a gap it does not fit. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| CR11 | Dynamic cabinet defaults (CB-475): without a per-field link flag, OK in the Cabinet Defaults dialog moves every standard base cabinet whose value still equals the OLD default (countertop thickness and overhangs, toe kick height and depth, backsplash height and thickness) to the new one; a value set to something else is kept. A thicker top raises the cabinet by the difference. Generated countertops follow through the slabs they remember. One undo step, 'Cabinet Defaults'. Brief 26's generic `use_default` flags replace this when they land. Set as Default (Edit toolbar of the Cabinet tool) copies one standard cabinet into the defaults of its kind and reports 'Base Cabinet Defaults have been updated'; it is refused for corner, blind, library-type and custom shapes. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| CR12 | Wall cabinet over an appliance (CB-635): a wall cabinet placed over a free-standing library appliance or the appliance bay of a cabinet takes its bottom from the appliance top when that top is above the wall cabinet's default bottom (a refrigerator, not a dishwasher), and its height shrinks to stay under the ceiling (not below 12 in). Only at placement. | As described; verify in Chief. | 2026-10-09 |
| CR13 | Plan Display Options: Show Closed Doors/Drawers and Panels draws each distinct door, drawer and panel width as a 3/4 in box at the front (stacked items of one width draw once); Show Pilasters draws the pilaster squares on the ends no merged neighbour shares (adjacent fronts share front pilasters); Display Molding Edges draws the countertop outline inset by the edge profile size. They are not on their own layers yet (CB-485). | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| CR14 | Extended stiles: `stile_ext_left` and `stile_ext_right` (inches) on `Cabinet` carry what the Box Construction panel's side extend will set; they shorten automatic fillers, add module-line frames and put XL, XR or XLR in the label. Brief 24 owns the dialog and may rename them. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| CR15 | Cabinet Schedule categories (L-57): `plan_cabinets::schedule_category` and `schedule_counts` name the seven categories (Base, Wall, Full Height, Fillers, Soffits, Shelves, Partitions); automatic fillers are never counted. `schedule_kinds.rs` (brief 04) still lists every cabinet in one table; the category column and grouping are queued. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| RB1 | Roof Groups (R-114, manual p. 826): `plan_roof::assign_roof_groups` splits a floor's rooms into the sets that share a roof. Group 0 keeps the plate-height rule of DECISIONS 118 (so no existing plan changes); each other group is one assignment at the tallest `room_level` among its rooms. Two groups at one height are two buildings. The brief said both "unset rooms are one integrated system" and "plate-height grouping stays as the default for unset groups"; the code keeps the second (conservative: nothing changes until a group is set). Where two groups touch, the shared wall gets no special treatment (both keep their overhang there): Chief says groups usually need hand editing and attic-wall changes afterwards. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| RB2 | Roof Baseline Polyline tool (Build > Roof > Roof Baseline Polyline) is a Plan Studio extra: Chief makes baselines only from Build Roof and edits them as CAD polylines; the tool draws one by hand (click corners, click the first corner or Enter to close, Esc cancels, a click on an existing baseline selects it, double-click opens the specification). | Kept as an extra. | 2026-10-09 |
| RB3 | Baseline storage: a closed CAD polyline on the layer "Roofs, Baseline Polylines" plus a `{"kind":"baseline","id":..}` record in `Floor.roofs` (Baseline Height and one directive per edge), keyed by the polyline id like tray ceilings. `roof_view` passes the record through untouched (an unknown kind). Directives are per edge index: a polyline that gains a corner gives the new edge the directive of the edge before it (`RoofBaseline::fit`); one that loses corners drops the tail. A record whose polyline is gone is ignored. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| RB4 | Baseline Height is one value per polyline, not per edge (the skeleton builder has one eave elevation per roof); Chief makes a separate polyline for every height too. The height is measured above the floor datum of the floor the polyline is on. Make Roof Baseline Polylines stores the height Build Roof would have used (the top plate plus Raise Roof Off Plate, and the structure thickness when the baseline rule is on). | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| RB5 | Directive text (own wording of p. 851): V against a wall, G a gable or shed edge (Full Gable, Dutch Gable, High Shed), K a knee wall, L a slope extended downward, then the pitch, or "(vert)" when no plane slopes toward the edge (Full Gable, High Shed, Against Wall). A plain hip shows its pitch alone. Knee Wall on a baseline is a plane that bears on a knee wall below (it rises like a hip). Against Wall is built as a High Shed/Gable Wall (no plane, no overhang). | As described; verify in Chief. | 2026-10-09 |
| RB6 | Make Roof Baseline Polylines is a one-shot switch (never stored): Auto Rebuild must not remake polylines over the user's edits. It deletes the build floor's and the wings' baselines and the unretained roof planes, and makes one polyline per roof region (wings at other plate heights get their own, on their own floor). Use Existing Roof Baselines persists in the roof settings; it builds only from the baselines (no flat or roofless-room handling, no auto returns) and the wings of lower floors from the baselines on those floors. Baseline edits do not trigger Auto Rebuild; rebuild by hand, as the manual says. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| RB7 | Retain switches default on. A rebuild has always kept every non-automatic plane (RF-6, RF-37), and Chief's manual does not give the dialog's stock state; keeping both on changes nothing for existing plans. "Manually drawn" is a plane with no source edge; "edited automatic" is a plane that was automatic (it has its source edge) and was moved, re-pitched or curved. The classification is `tools::roof_baseline::retained`. | As described; verify in Chief. | 2026-10-09 |
| RB8 | Coplanar rule (p. 829): a new plane is dropped when every vertex stands within 0.5 in of a retained plane's surface, the normals are within a quarter of a degree, and the plan overlap is at least half the area of the smaller of the two ("at least half the area of either of them" is read as either one, which is the more inclusive reading and drops a small new plane inside a big retained one). Applied to the build floor and to the wings. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| RB9 | Curved walls (p. 826): `plan_roof::flatten_curved_walls` replaces each curved wall by straight sections of at most the Segment Angle (default 15 degrees, limited to 6 to 90) before the footprint and the edge directives are worked out; the sections keep the wall's thickness, height and roof directive. A concave wall (one that bows into the building) is cut only when its sections are longer than the Minimum Alcove Size (default 24 in); otherwise it is spanned by its straight baseline (the manual's second simplification; the first, extending the neighbours, is not offered). `wall_signature` includes the bulge so Auto Rebuild reacts. | As described; defaults to verify in Chief. | 2026-10-09 |
| RB10 | Pitch in Degrees (p. 829) is a per-plan switch kept in the roof settings and mirrored to a per-thread flag (`plan_roof::pitch_display_degrees`) that `pitch_label` and the pitch boxes read; loading a roof with settings sets it. The manual allows -89 to 89 degrees; the boxes accept 1 to 89 (a plane below 0.5 in 12 or sloping down is not a roof plane here). | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| RB11 | Curved plane (pp. 847, 854 to 857): the section across the slope is a circular arc through the eave and the ridge edge; the three numbers follow the manual (angle at eave + angle at ridge = 2 x pitch angle; radius from the turn and the chord). The spec stores the two angles and the facet settings; the radius is worked out from the plane's run. Facets are strips parallel to the eave cut at equal angle steps (7.5 degrees by default, or the set angle moved to the nearest divisor of 360), each a planar plane on the arc. A curved plane has no holes or skylights cut in 3D, and its eave details still follow the flat chord plane (`RoofCover`, brief 18's file). Editing the curve makes an automatic plane edited. Straighten Curved Edge is not built. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| RB12 | Join Curved Roof Plane: when the ridge edge of a curved plane moves to meet another plane its run changes, the chord keeps its pitch, and the curve follows. Lock the Radius keeps the radius (the angles at ridge and eave change to fit); Lock the Angle at Ridge keeps the angle at the ridge (the radius and the eave angle change). A locked radius too short for the new chord refuses the join. The Join Roof Planes tool opens the dialog only for a curved plane. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| RB13 | Show All Ridges (p. 832) defaults on (what was always drawn). Off, plan view leaves out the hips between neighbouring automatic planes of one pitch whose eaves turn by no more than the segment angle, which is how the sections of a curved wall's roof look; it cannot tell them from a very gentle corner of a straight wall. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| AG1 | Plan Agent floor numbers: the tools number floors 1.. in stack order, exactly the index of `Project::floors` plus one. With a Foundation floor built, floor 1 is the Foundation (the UI also lists it first); the plan summary and `get_plan_summary` print every floor's number, name and kind so the model never has to guess. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| AG2 | Plan Agent exterior walls: `add_wall_rectangle` draws counter-clockwise and sets `exterior_side` to the right so the exterior face points outward; `add_wall` leaves the side at the default (left), so the system prompt tells the model to draw exterior outlines clockwise. The wall tool itself never sets the side automatically, so nothing else can be copied. | As described; verify in Chief. | 2026-10-09 |
| AG3 | Plan Agent tool catalog has 23 tools, all `strict: true` (per the brief). If the API caps strict tools per request (20 at the time of writing, unverified for the 2026-10 API) and answers 400, set `strict` false in `ToolDef::to_api_json` for the least important tools; nothing else depends on it. | Strict on all 23. | 2026-10-09 |
| AG4 | Plan Agent history is atomic per run: the messages of a run are appended to the session only when the run finishes (end of turn, token cap or tool-round cap). A failed, refused or cancelled run discards its messages and its plan edits, so the history never shows the model tool results for an edit the editor did not keep. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| AG5 | Plan Agent walls are joined by position only (ends within 0.5 in); plan-core has no connection helper (that lives in `plan-app` `editor/connect.rs`). The app should run Fix Wall Connections on the project returned by `AgentEvent::Finished` when `changed` is true, so T-junctions get split and corners joined the way a hand-drawn plan is. | Left to the app. | 2026-10-09 |
| AG6 | `validate_plan` calls a wall end closed when another wall has an end within 1 in or its centerline passes within 1 in of the end (a T-junction). Chief's own rule for open rooms is the room detection result, so a closed end that still bounds no room shows up as `rooms: 0` rather than an unclosed end. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| AG7 | Plan Agent doors on exterior walls swing inward and the hinge goes to the jamb nearer the closer wall end (Chief's pointer rule with the pointer on the room side); a door on an interior wall keeps the default swing. Opening sizes come from the plan's exterior/interior door and window defaults, and the style's own size from the opening variant defaults when a style is named. | As described; verify in Chief. | 2026-10-09 |
| RB14 | The question message when a roof plane is edited or drawn (RF-69, manual p. 827) appears while Auto Rebuild Roofs is ON in the manual; the brief said "off". The manual was followed in the queue note, but the prompt itself is not built (no tool-level yes/no prompt exists and `tools/roof.rs` belongs to another builder). Editing stays allowed; the Retain switches (RB7) protect edited and manual planes through a rebuild. | Judgment call; verify in Chief. | 2026-10-09 |
| CP1 | Common object pages (round 16 brief 05, L-74, L-75, R-89, R-90): the Label, Schedule and Manufacturer panels live in `Project.props.pages[<Property Manager key>]` (`plan_core::object_pages::ObjectPages`, only non-default panels stored, so old plans load unchanged). The shared frame adds them as tabs after Components / Object Information for the kinds that take them (`common_pages::Kind`); a dialog that already has a tab of that name (an opening's Label and Schedule, a symbol's or cabinet's) keeps it and the frame appends the shared parts it lacks under it (offsets, angle, label layer, callout rotation), so no field appears twice. No dialog needs code to opt in; the elevation widget is one line (`elevation_ref::row`). Chief shows one panel per dialog; two sections in one tab is the conservative way to keep the existing dialogs untouched. | Appended sections | 2026-10-09 |
| CP2 | Shared plan labels draw only for objects with a stored Label panel; objects that already label themselves on the plan (doors, windows, cabinets, symbols, devices, rooms, roof planes) get a second label only from a custom (Specify) label, never from the Automatic Label, so nothing is drawn twice. Suppress Label in All Views on a self-labelling object is stored but still needs that object's own label renderer to read it (integration-queue). | Custom only | 2026-10-09 |
| CP3 | Elevation Reference (R-89, manual p. 21): the stored number keeps its meaning as the distance typed in the dialog; the reference says what it is measured from (Absolute, From Floor, From Finished Floor, From Terrain, From Ceiling, From Roof) and To Top / To Bottom says which edge it reaches. The resolved bottom is `datum + value` (To Bottom) or `datum + value - height` (To Top), so a top 6 in below the ceiling is typed as -6 (Chief's sign convention for From Ceiling / From Roof is not documented in the manual; verify in Chief). From Terrain without terrain is From Floor; From Roof without a roof is From Ceiling. The default (From Floor, To Bottom) is not stored and not resolved, so old plans never move, including in a room with a Floor Height offset. | Distance with sign | 2026-10-09 |
| CP4 | The 3D scene applies references through `elevation_ref::effective_project`, a copy of the plan with each referenced opening sill, symbol elevation, cabinet elevation and device height rewritten, built only when some object chose a reference. The plan view, elevations and schedules still show the typed numbers. `Cabinet.elevation_ref` (the field added by the cabinet brief) is no longer edited by the Cabinet Specification; the shared widget replaces its combo. | Copy for the scene | 2026-10-09 |
| CP5 | Object Information (Code, Comment, Description, Manufacturer, Supplier) typed in the shared tab replaces the matching cell of any schedule row for that object when it is not empty, over the dialog's own fields (an opening's Schedule tab, a wall's info); Include in Schedule cleared on the Schedule panel removes the row. The native `extras.spec.schedule.include` of openings and `SymbolSchedule.exclude` keep working beside it. | Overlay | 2026-10-09 |
| CP6 | The window Automatic Label of the Label panel preview uses the type code the brief asks for (3040 DH: `object_pages::opening_automatic_label`, Include Type appends DH, CS, SL, ...), but `Opening::plan_label` (plan-core openings.rs, brief 22) still puts the style name first ("Window 3050", tested). The two should be unified by the openings owner. | Open | 2026-10-09 |
| LA1 | Migration of the single thicknesses to one-layer definitions (R-27..R-29, R-123) is virtual: a slot that was never laid out in layers (`AssemblySlot::Legacy`) reads as a one-layer definition made from the old field (`plan_core::assemblies::resolve`), and nothing is written for it, so an older plan loads, shows and saves byte for byte as before. The brief asked for a migration on load; writing one-layer definitions into every old file would have changed every old plan on its first save. A layered slot writes its resolved total back into the old field (`Project::sync_platform_mirrors`, run on load and after every dialog) so floor-to-floor rise, wall tops and the other readers of the old fields stay right. | Virtual migration, mirrors for layered slots; verify in Chief that old plans show the same thickness. | 2026-10-09 |
| LA2 | Where the default chain lives (R-124): plan-wide is `Project::assemblies.plan_wide` (kept in the plan, edited on Default Settings > Floor/Ceiling Platform), the floor level is `FloorSettings::platform`, the room is `RoomMisc::assemblies`; each slot is Legacy, Use Default or its own definition. A floor that uses the default holds a copy of the plan-wide definition (`platform_inherited`, refreshed by `sync_thicknesses`) so the 3D builders, which see only the floor, need no plan-wide lookup. When the plan-wide definition is first set, a floor still at the thickness the plan-wide level had before starts following it (Chief floors follow their defaults unless changed); a floor changed by hand keeps its own. The room-type level of the chain (manual p. 770) and Joist Direction override are not built (R-124 stays Partial). | As described; verify in Chief. | 2026-10-09 |
| LA3 | Layer order: every definition lists its highest layer first, as a section view does (the manual's dropped ceiling is plenum, framing, drywall, paint from the top, p. 768). The older Ceiling Structure stack was listed from the underside up; it is turned around when read as a definition (`Assembly::from_structure_of`) and when mirrored back. | Top first; verify in Chief that Ceiling Structure lists the same way. | 2026-10-09 |
| LA4 | Dropped ceiling (manual p. 768; the manual gives the layers but not the heights): the Ceiling Height and the wall tops stay; the finished ceiling hangs lower by the Air Gap and Framing layers of the Ceiling Finish (`Assembly::drop`) and the platform's underside is the Ceiling Height plus the other (surface) layers, which is exactly the old rule when a finish has no plenum or framing. `RoomLevels::ceiling_height` is the finished (hung) ceiling, so crown molding, the ceiling plate and tray ceilings follow it. A Rough Ceiling (R-25) still sets the platform's underside; the gap above the finish is solid. | As described; verify in Chief how a dropped ceiling's height is entered. | 2026-10-09 |
| LA5 | DECISIONS 305 updated: a ceiling platform is built from the room's layered Ceiling Structure and Ceiling Finish when it has them (one slice per layer, an Air Gap drawn empty); the single solid block remains only for a platform with no layered definition, so plans without layers are unchanged. | Layered where defined, one block otherwise. | 2026-10-09 |
| LA6 | Layered floors in 3D: the Floor Structure layers hang below the platform datum (the old block hung from the finished floor), the Floor Finish layers stand on it, so a 7/8 in tile and backer finish raises the finished floor by 7/8 in and a 12 in structure lowers the platform's underside by 12 in. A platform with a layered finish but an older structure (or the reverse) keeps its older block on its side of the datum. Layer materials come from the layer name's keyword, else its role (framing and sheathing are lumber, a plain layer in a finish is concrete-coloured bedding). | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| LA7 | Default Settings > Floors and Rooms > Floor/Ceiling Platform opens its own page (`Leaf::Platforms`, `dialogs/assembly_def.rs`) because the plan-wide definitions belong to the plan (one undo step), not to the template file. The generic `platforms` page spec in `default_pages/plan.rs` stays registered with the four single-thickness fields but no tree leaf reaches it. | Own page; the generic spec is unused. | 2026-10-09 |
| LA8 | Changing a room's type resets its Floor Structure and Floor Finish to the type's own platform (a garage's slab, a deck's boards), dropping a layered definition made before, because the type's function sets those two; Ceiling Structure and Ceiling Finish are kept. | As described. | 2026-10-09 |
| LA9 | Roof layers (RF-36, DECISIONS 122): a single plane's Structure panel has Roof Surface, Roof Structure and Roof Ceiling Finish Edit buttons (Material Layers Definition). The plane keeps the layers (`RoofPlaneRecord::layers`, JSON `layers`) and derives the numbers the 3D roof and framing read (`RoofLayers::numbers`: framing layer depth, width and spacing; Sheathing layers; everything else over the framing counts as roofing). The all-planes Edit sheet still uses the numeric Define window, and setting a structure that way drops a plane's layers. Purlins (framing method) and 3D Cladding (role) are stored on the layers and read by nothing yet. | Single plane layered; bulk edit numeric. | 2026-10-09 |
| LA10 | Materials List (R-27..R-29): a room with a layered platform lists each layer: Sheathing of a floor structure as Subfloor (4x8 sheets), Finish layers of a floor finish as Flooring and plain layers under them as Underlayment (square feet), Framing layers as floor or ceiling framing (square feet of the room with the member and spacing in the description), drywall of a ceiling finish as Wallboard (4x8 sheets); an Air Gap is no purchase. A platform with no layered definition keeps the Flooring and ceiling drywall rows it always had. Board feet of the framing layers are not counted (the framing tools own lumber). | As described. | 2026-10-09 |

| MO1 | Molding profile frame (round 16, brief 31, `plan_core::moldings`): a profile is one or more closed polylines at actual size with the back of the molding on the vertical edge x = 0, the section projecting toward +x and its bottom at y = 0, counter-clockwise. The manual does not say which edge is the back; verify in Chief. | Left edge is the back | 2026-10-09 |

| MO6 | Joints: edges are mitred in the plane that bisects the two directions, also for edges that rise and fall; at a twisted joint the two sections are averaged (Auto Calc Orientation at Twisted Joints), kept as the previous edge has them (auto-calc off) or cut square (Mitre Molding at Twisted Joints off). Mitre Molding If Next Edge Turned Off cuts the end on the mitre plane of the edge that is off. Corners sharper than the bisector limit are cut off at four times the section's projection. | Bisector mitre | 2026-10-09 |
| MO7 | Room molding sources, in order: the room's own table, else its room type's table, else the Floor Defaults table; porches, decks, garages, stairwells, Open Below rooms and courtyards get nothing from the Floor Defaults (manual p. 462). Wiring it into `plan-docs`' Materials List engine is not done (not an owned file; integration queue). | Function ready, engine hook open | 2026-10-09 |
| RT1 | Gable/Roof Line objects (round 16, brief 20, RF-44, RF-133..RF-136): a line is a record of `Floor.roofs` (`{"kind": "gable_line"}`, `tools::gable_line`) kept until it is deleted; every Build Roof turns the lines into gables again (`apply_stored`), so the earlier "click an eave" Gable/Roof Line tool and the new object coexist. It must be exactly parallel to an exterior wall (cross product of the unit directions under 0.01, about half a degree) and within ten feet of the wall's Main Layer, taken as the whole wall thickness from the centre line; a line on the Main Layer is allowed (that is how a gable over an opening sits). The gable has two planes of the line's pitch and overhang: their eaves run away from the line at the roof's eave height, the ridge stands over the middle, the rake overhang goes the overhang past each end and past the line; the gable runs from the line into the roof until its ridge meets the roof surface, and the roof keeps what is higher than the gable (valleys where they cross). Which way the gable runs is decided by the roof: the side where the roof is deeper. Gable Over Door/Window lines lie on the outside face, 12 in past each side, merged within 30 in (manual p. 863); the line remembers the openings it covers for Delete Gable Over Opening. | 12 in / 30 in as the manual says; default pitch and overhang are the Build Roof settings (8:12, 16 in); verify in Chief | 2026-10-09 |
| RT2 | Roof trim parts (round 16, brief 20, RF-83..RF-87, RF-156): generated as ordinary `MoldingLine`s of the floor's details layer on the layer "Roofs, Trim", Automatically Generated on, label "Roof Trim: <part>" (rafter tail, ridge cap, gutter, frieze, shadow board, subfascia, lookout), profile named "<part> - <library profile>". `MoldingSource` has no roof variant and `plan-core/src/details.rs` belongs to the moldings owner, so the layer and the label say where a line came from. They list under Exterior Trim in the Materials List through `trim_takeoff`. A line edited by hand loses Automatic; a later Build Roof Trim keeps it and does not make a twin where its bounds (12 in slack) hold the new line's middle. | Layer and label identify roof trim; no new MoldingSource | 2026-10-09 |
| RT3 | Ridge caps (RF-84): with Bend to Roof Pitch the cap is one strip on each plane of the ridge or hip, lying on its surface (a tilted rectangle as the custom section); without it one level strip centred on the line. The width is the whole cap across both planes. The Ridge Cap setting of an edge: Automatic caps ridges and hips, On adds any edge, Off removes; on a shared edge Off on either side wins, then On. | 10 in wide, 3/4 in thick, bent | 2026-10-09 |
| RT4 | Boxed and Flush eaves (RF-79, RF-155): the manual names them without defining them. Boxed = a horizontal soffit under the fascia, Flush = a soffit that follows the underside of the rafters; Higher Eaves Boxed boxes the eaves above the roof's lowest eave even when the style is Flush. Trim Framing To Soffits is stored (`RoofTrimOptions::trim_framing_to_soffits`) for the framing builder, which does not read it yet. The soffit surfaces themselves are still `plan_3d::eave` elements driven by `RoofDetail::soffit` and `sloped_soffit`; `soffit_boxed` is the rule the app should use to set them per eave. | Boxed; verify in Chief | 2026-10-09 |
| RT5 | Rafter tail recipes (RF-83, RF-158): Exposed = tails show over the whole overhang and no soffit closes them in (whatever the soffit style), Hidden = no tails (a soffit covers the overhang), Partially Exposed = the last `exposed_length` inches show past a soffit at the wall. Extend Past Subfascia pushes the tail out beyond the eave line by that much, continuing the roof's underside slope. Stretch to Fit Rafter takes the roof's rafter width and depth. | Exposed, stretched | 2026-10-09 |
| RT6 | Inside Hole Rim (RF-141): Square = walls square to the roof surface (the walls along the slope are vertical, the head and sill lean up the slope by drop x pitch), Plumb = every wall vertical, Plumb/Square = plumb sill (low side), square head (high side). The manual lists the three names without drawings, so which side is plumb in Plumb/Square is a guess. | Square; verify in Chief | 2026-10-09 |
| RT7 | Skylight options that the roof model cannot hold (shape, size, rim, ceiling hole, Display in Plan View) are `skylight_options` records of `Floor.roofs`, found by the roof plane and the centroid of the hole (1 in); a skylight that moved loses them. Chimneys and up-slope walls do not get one automatically yet. | Half the roof pitch | 2026-10-09 |
| RT9 | Gambrel dormer (RF-146, RF-147): a gable dormer whose planes break at In from Eave from each eave, steeper below (`DormerSpec::pitch`) and `pitch2` above; the ridge height follows from the two pitches and `height_to_ridge` is ignored. Mansard, Barrel, Curved Eave, Hip Curved Eave and Eyebrow dormers are not built. `DormerSpec` is not extended (it has literals in other crates); the second pitch is the separate `SecondPitch`. | Gambrel only | 2026-10-09 |
| RT10 | Auto Roof Return on gable dormers (RF-150): a return at each front eave corner of the finished (overhanging) dormer roof, wrapping the corner along the eave and up the front rake; hip and shed dormers get none. | RM1 | Room functions (Round 16 brief 15, R-101, manual pp. 446 and 447): a function is a fixed property set in `plan_core::rooms` (`ROOM_FUNCTIONS`, `function_defaults_with`): Interior = Standard, Utility; Exterior = Balcony, Court, Deck; Hybrid = Attic, Garage, Open Below, Porch, Slab. Basement and Crawl Space are no longer functions (DECISIONS 300 corrected): `canonical_function` maps the older names (Basement to Standard, Crawl Space to Open Below, Living to Standard); the type names Attic, Courtyard and Crawl Space still imply Attic, Court and Open Below whatever function an older list names, and the Basement type still gets its slab and ceiling by name. The embedded seed list (`defaults.rs chief_room_types` and `assets/templates/chief-x18-daniel.json`, kept equal by a test) now names Attic, Balcony, Courtyard (Court), Crawl Space (Open Below) and Slab by their function, and Garage is unconditioned and Slab out of the Living Area, as the manual's defaults say. | Functions as in the manual | 2026-10-09 |
| RM2 | What a function sets (R-101): Interior = living, conditioned, flat ceiling, roof over; Exterior = no ceiling, no roof, no foundation, doors and windows face out; Hybrid = out of the Living Area and the Conditioned Area (Open Below is conditioned); Garage, Slab and Porch keep a ceiling and a roof; an Attic has no floor, no ceiling and no roof (the roof generator ignores it); a Slab drops to Floor 0 like a Garage with a floor platform as thick as the Foundation Defaults slab (`foundation.slab_thickness`, 4 in); Balcony has the Deck platform (decking on joists) without a Deck Specification; Court has a 4 in slab and, as the Courtyard type since Round 13, no floor when the type is the Courtyard. `apply_function_defaults` now also sets Roof Over This Room, Flat Ceiling Over This Room and Build Foundation Below. | LV1 | Update on Demand and Plot Lines pictures. A layout box sent as a cross section, elevation or camera view keeps a picture of the view's vector drawing (`BoxView::art`: edge and pattern lines, cut, shadow and material fills, annotation text) when its Camera View Option is Update on Demand or Plot Lines; Live View, Always Update keeps none and draws the plan as it is (dynamic). Update on Demand pictures are made again when their page prints (`layout_window::print_bytes` / `print_with` update a copy, never the layout) and by Update View / Update All Views / Update All Live Views; Plot Lines pictures only by Update View / Update All Views / Update All Plot Line Views. Chief draws a Live View's picture with the view's rendering technique and refreshes it by re-rendering; Plan Studio's elevations are vector drawings already, so the kept picture is the same drawing frozen. Plan views are dynamic in Plan Studio (they redraw from the plan), perspective boxes stay semi-dynamic through Update Views' render thread, and pictures (`ImageData`) are static. | Vector drawing kept as the layout view's picture | 2026-10-09 |
| LV2 | Plot line extraction. The generated plot lines are the lines of the hidden-line drawing the Live View prints (`plan_elevation::elevation` / `section`, a camera's `camera_drawing`): lines of kind Hatch (and the scene's wall-face hatch strokes) are Pattern lines in their material's color, everything else is an Edge line with the vector view's weight class (heavy, medium, light, cut, hidden dashed). Edge lines are black unless Use Edge Line Defaults is on; pattern lines take their material color unless Use Pattern Line Defaults is on; a line's own weight, style or color (Layout Line Specification) beats both. Color Fill paints each visible face region in its material's color under the lines; the cut poche and shadows are always gray fills. Dimensions in camera views, images and reference models are not drawn (the manual leaves them out of Plot Lines); CAD and text drawn in the cross section stay live, which Plan Studio does not yet draw in elevation boxes at all. | Edge/pattern split by line kind; Color Fill by face region | 2026-10-09 |
| LV3 | Fit to Sheet (No Scale) sends the view at the factor that fits half the width and half the height of the page's drawing area (the manual says 'approximately half the size of the layout drawing sheet'); the box has no scale and reads NOT TO SCALE. Resizing it by a corner handle with the Alternate edit behavior (the Command key) resizes the view with the border (`plan_layout::resize_no_scale`); every other handle only crops. Scale Layout Box Contents to Fit chooses the exact factor that fills the box (a scale on no list is fine, shown as 1:n when the ratio is whole, else inches per foot). | Half width and half height; Command is the Alternate behavior | 2026-10-09 |
| LV4 | Use Layout Line Scaling (default on) keeps pen weights as drawn on the sheet at any scale. Switched off, weights grow and shrink with the view against 1/4 in = 1 ft (the scale the plan's own pens are made for); Chief scales against the original view's scale, which a Plan Studio box does not record. The vector view's pen weights are 0.18, 0.35 and 0.7 pt and the cut line 1.0 pt at 1/4 in. | Reference scale 1/4 in = 1 ft | 2026-10-09 |
| LV5 | Missing layout views. A box is flagged (a caution triangle with the reason at its top left corner on the page, in print and on screen; not part of its own lines) when its floor is gone, its linked saved plan view is gone or that view's layer set is missing, or its camera is missing. A box's own layer set string is a label as much as a link (plans are sent with the name of the layer display they were drawn with and `All` ignores visibility), so only the layer set of a linked saved plan view is checked. A camera box is only flagged when the application's drawing hook is installed and has no drawing for it, so headless use never shows a caution. Ignore Invalid Links (Layout Box Specification > Linked View) hides the symbol. Refresh Link and Relink to a different plan file do not apply (one plan, one layout inside the project); queued in the integration queue. | Caution symbol outside the box's own lines | 2026-10-09 |
| LV6 | Link Saved Plan View. A plan box can name a saved plan view (`BoxView::saved_view`); it then takes that view's floor (when the view has one) and layer set, so changing the saved view changes the box. The Send to Layout dialog starts the check box on while the plan has an active saved plan view. Unlink Saved Plan View copies the view's floor and layer set into the box and clears the link (one undo step). Create Saved Plan View (make a view from a box) is queued. The Default Set, Show Color, Reference Display and Poche settings of an unsaved plan view are stored on the box (`BoxView`); plan boxes draw Poche and Show Color only where the renderer already supports them (not yet), so they are recorded and edited but not applied. | Saved view supplies floor and layer set | 2026-10-09 |
| LV7 | Box label. `%scale%`, `%view_name%`, `%view_type%` and `%layout_page_label%` are our names for the layout box's object-specific macros (the manual shows the idea, not the names); the callout macros `%linked_view_name%`, `%linked_view_layout_page_label%`, `%referenced_view_callout_label%` and `%layout_page_label%` of the callouts builder (`plan_core::callout::expand_macros`) work in labels that carry a link. A link to a layout page reports the page's own label from Page Information (`A5.1`), not the plain sheet number the callout code reads from the JSON. The label sits on the new Layout Box Labels layer (a sixth layout layer; older layouts gain it when loaded). The label's callout or marker shape is drawn with `CalloutShape` outlines around the callout text; the six positions are bottom or top, left, center or right; the scale note goes under (or over) the label and reads NOT TO SCALE for a box with no scale. | Our macro names; page label from Page Information | 2026-10-09 |
| LV8 | Layout CAD parity (item 6). The layout window has its own select path over page CAD, leaders, clouds and boxes (`shell/layout_window.rs`); the plan's `tools/select.rs` works on a floor's objects, so hooking one into the other would copy page model code. Instead the layout got what the manual lists as commands of its own: object snaps (corners and ends of page CAD and boxes win over the grid, Alt turns snapping off), Center Object (the selection to the middle of the drawing area), and Point to Point Move (a tool: click from, click to). Concentric copies, Selected Edge dimensions on page drawings and Fillet remain queued. Text in a turned view already turns with it (the box turns its text prims with the contents). | Own commands in the layout window, not select.rs | 2026-10-09 |
| LV9 | Camera View Options and Current Screen As Image. As Image embeds a 150 dpi raster of the view's lines (`plan_layout::send_as_image`) as a static picture box sized as the live view would be; rendered (perspective) views are always pictures. Current Screen needs the part of the plan on screen, which the application's canvas owns: `layout_window::set_plan_screen` takes it, and until `main.rs` calls it Current Screen sends the whole view (the dialog says so). Send All Remaining Views sends every waiting view with the same answers (floors and elevation cameras for Send All Views). | Raster at 150 dpi; screen extent through a hook | 2026-10-09 |
| LV10 | Site-plan scales. Send to Layout, the Box Scale panel, Change Scale and Pan/Scale offer `Scale::choices()` (the architectural list, 1 in = 30, 40, 50, 60 and 100 ft, then the metric ratios) and a typed scale: `1:240`, `1200`, `1/8" = 1'`, `1 in = 80 ft`, `1 m = 50 m` (`plan_layout::parse_scale_text`). 1 in = 100 ft is exactly 1:1200 on paper. Largest-that-fits steps through the architectural scales and on through the site scales, so a large lot ends at 1 in = 100 ft. A view too big for the drawing area at the chosen scale is still sent, with the warning 'The view is too big for the sheet' in the dialog and in the status line. | Typed ratio accepted in Send, Change Scale and Pan/Scale | 2026-10-09 |
| SC1 | Schedule Specification General panel (L-233, manual pp. 719 to 720): Main Title with Display, Include Objects from All Floors, a floor list, a room list (rooms have no id, so a room is the one that holds a stored point; `RoomRef`), Select All and Clear All, and the Categories to Include tree. The tree lists, per kind, the categories the plan declares and those its objects use (door and window styles; wall types; room types; the seven cabinet categories and Other; electrical device kinds; Plumbing, Appliances, HVAC and Lighting for fixtures, told apart by the symbol's library category and name; a plant is one category; stair shapes; note types), with the custom categories last. A schedule that never recorded a choice lists everything; Wall, Room and Note types made later join a schedule that takes new types (Wall and Room schedules do, Note schedules do not, and a new Note schedule records its types as ticked). Unticking is by category id (`Wall/Siding-6`). Judgement: the fixture and appliance split is by keywords (verify in Chief). | Tree from the plan | 2026-10-09 |
| SC2 | Include Objects from Room (L-60, manual p. 709): an object is listed when its centre point is inside the room's centerline polygon or on its edge, so a door in the partition between two rooms is listed in both. Distributed objects (paths and regions) are not built. Create Schedule from Room sets one room, This Floor, and a title of the room's name and the kind; the Schedule tool places it with the next click. | Centerline polygon | 2026-10-09 |
| SC3 | Custom Schedule Categories (L-61, manual pp. 710 to 711) live in `Project::schedule_setup`; an object joins one by its property key (`door:12`) from Manage Custom Schedule Categories (Add Selection) or from its own Schedule panel. A schedule that ticks a custom category lists those objects whatever their kind, in addition to its own kind, with the fields they share. Renaming follows into every schedule; deleting drops the tick. | Setup in the plan | 2026-10-09 |
| SC4 | Columns/Rows (L-28, L-69, manual pp. 716 to 722). Group Similar Objects is off by default here (Chief: on) so every existing plan and test keeps one row per object; with it on, objects whose shown columns (not Number, Quantity, previews or Sum Similar columns) are equal share a row and a number, and the Quantity column counts them. Calculate Total and Sum Similar Rows work on the columns that hold a count, length, area or volume; a Sum Similar column does not tell objects apart. The Totals row appears when a column calculates a total (the Area columns of Door, Window and Room Finish and the Volume of Room Finish do by default), adds the values of the rows, carries the label in the first column unless that column totals, and stays with the last wrapped table. Minimum Rows pads above the Totals row. The old count line and Group by column stay for existing schedules. | Off by default | 2026-10-09 |
| SC5 | Number Formatting (L-72, manual pp. 724 to 725): a column's format is optional; none keeps the plan's own length text. Values are formatted from the numbers the schedule keeps (`Entry::nums`), not from the cell text. Diagonal and Vertical fractions are set in the view: the fraction in smaller text raised and lowered along a slash, or stacked over a line; the cell text and the exports keep `1/4`. | Format per column | 2026-10-09 |
| SC6 | Wrapping and Swap Rows/Columns (L-64, L-71, manual pp. 712, 722): `table()` is the data, one row per object, and is what CSV and Excel export; Swap and Wrapping are the view's (`schedule_view::layout`). Entries per Table fills tables in order; Max Table Size fills each table until the next row would pass the size (a table holds at least one row); the Totals row ends the last table. Swapped, the objects are columns headed by their numbers and the attributes run down the first column; Display Column Headings off drops that column. Tables sit side by side (stacked when swapped) a Wrapped Schedule Offset apart; Justify stretches rows (columns) to one size. The Wrap handle drag sets Max Table Size and turns Wrapping on without Chief's confirmation (verify in Chief). The Scale button of the size field is left out: sizes are plan inches. | Data apart from view | 2026-10-09 |
| SC7 | Object previews (L-234, manual pp. 717, 723): Callout Symbol, 2D Symbol, 3D Elevation and 3D Perspective are small vector pictures of the object's size and kind, not plot-line renders of the 3D model; Scale Images shares one scale per column; Show Color paints from the plan with one colour per kind or from the schedule's line colour; Use Plan View Scale and the opening indicator, casing and treatment options are stored but the pictures do not change for them (verify in Chief). | Simple pictures | 2026-10-09 |
| SC8 | Edit handles (L-63, manual p. 712): Move, side Resize, Rotate, Resize Column, Move Row, Move Column, Sort by Column and Wrap work from the Schedule tool and from Select Objects; each drag is one undo step. Side Resize changes the right-most column's width (the left handle keeps the right edge); Rotate snaps to 15 degrees within two. Move Row and Move Column act on release. A row move with Automatic Sorting on asks first (Turn Off Sorting or Cancel) and is one undo step with the sort cleared. A click on a row selects it for Open Row Object(s) and Find Object in Plan. | Handles in both tools | 2026-10-09 |
| SC9 | Wall legend columns (L-235): Total Width is the thickest assembly of the wall's upper and lower parts (else its thickness); Wall Construction, Upper and Lower list the layers of the wall type, exterior face first, as `Material thickness` (a pony wall has its upper and lower types, a glass pony wall only a lower one, a plain wall the same for both). | From the wall types | 2026-10-09 |
| SC10 | Find Object in Plan (S-118) goes to the floor of the objects behind a row, selects them (a room as a room) and asks the plan to centre on them (`schedule_view::take_focus`, taken by `schedule_spec::show_extras`). Find Schedule(s) from Object (S-119) selects the schedule when one lists the object and opens Select Location (`dialogs/select_location.rs`: a list of locations with counts, OK goes there) when several do. Move Up / Down in Schedule is offered for an object in exactly one schedule. Find Wall (S-120) is `schedule_view::find_wall(wall id)` for the framing views to call; Find Trusses selects the trusses of the floor with the same truss detail as the selected one. | Row to objects | 2026-10-09 |
| SC11 | Labels panel (L-73, manual pp. 726 to 728): Use Both Callout and Label, Use Callout and Use Label; prefix, start number, number style (1, A, a, I, i) and leading zeros; ten callout shapes (a circle for doors and a hexagon for windows when none is picked); fill, colour, transparency, automatic size, shape and text angles, Follow Label; Callout Layer (object label layer, the schedule's layer, or a named layer). An object listed in two schedules shows a callout for each. Use Callout hiding the object's own plan label is not wired into the opening and cabinet labels (integration-queue). | Shapes drawn | 2026-10-09 |
| SC12 | Schedule Defaults (L-58, manual p. 708): the Default Settings > Schedules leaves open the same dialog as the Schedule Specification without Position and the output buttons; OK stores the schedule in `Project::schedule_setup` as the template new schedules of that kind start from (columns, widths, formats, categories, wrapping, attributes, labels; not the rooms or numbers). Double-clicking the Schedule Tools button is not wired (toolbar.rs). | Template per kind | 2026-10-09 |
| SC13 | Cabinet Schedule (L-57): the fillers the program makes are never listed, a Category column names the cabinet's schedule category (Base Cabinets, Wall Cabinets, ..., or Other for the rest). The Cabinet Accessories and Molding categories stay open. | Auto fillers left out | 2026-10-09 |
| EL1 | Electrical Defaults heights (round 16 brief 25, E-11, E-20; corrects the per-kind heights of 87 and 89): four groups and a Use Default Heights switch replace one height per kind. Outlet (every receptacle including WP, plus phone, data and TV jacks) 12", Switch (switches, doorbells, thermostats) 48", Above Base Cabinet measured up from the counter top (8", the old 44" counter outlet over a 36" counter) and On Cabinet Side measured up from the cabinet's bottom (32", manual p. 693), kept on the box. The WP outlet no longer has an 18" default of its own: it is an outlet (manual p. 692 lists one Outlet height); `DeviceKind::default_height` (18") is still the height saved with the symbol, used when Use Default Heights is off. A plan saved with the old record (`heights` keyed by kind name plus `Counter Outlet`) reads into the groups on load (110V Outlet to Outlet, Switch to Switch, Counter Outlet minus 36" to Above Base Cabinet) and writes the new record only when edited. | Per-kind heights | 2026-10-09 |
| EL2 | Sink base outlets (E-20): the manual says an outlet behind a sink base is at "the standard 11 1/2 in". The code keeps its 12" Outlet default (E-3, DECISIONS 87) and treats the sink case as "not over a counter": the plain Outlet height. A cabinet counts as a sink base when its face holds a Sink or its top has a sink hole. Verify in Chief whether the standard height is 11 1/2 in. | 12 in Outlet | 2026-10-09 |
| EL3 | Default Library Objects (E-19): the Library button chooses among the built-in types of a tool's family (the Light tool: ceiling, recessed, pendant; 110V: duplex or quad; 220V: 220V or dedicated; Switch: single-pole or dimmer; Wall Light and Wall Light (Exterior) separately), not Chief catalog objects, which place as symbols without circuits or heights (E-26). The Edit button of the list is disabled. The choice is stored as `ElectricalDefaults::objects` (tool name to `DeviceKind`) and is what the tool places; weatherproof types stay automatic. | Fixed kinds | 2026-10-09 |
| EL4 | Click-location rules of the outlet, switch and Light tools (E-30, E-34..E-36): a cabinet or soffit side wins over a wall only when it is nearer to the click than the wall face; cabinet sides standing against a wall are not offered. A face is outdoors when the point 4 in past it is in an exterior-type room, or in no room on an exterior wall. Over the counter of a Kitchen or Bath room a 110V outlet becomes a GFCI. In the open: outlets go on the floor (the 220V tool keeps its 220V receptacle), 110V in a Garage or Slab room on the ceiling, a weatherproof floor outlet on a deck or outside, a ceiling light (the soffit's bottom inside a soffit) or, outside every room, a path light; a switch is refused. Symbols carry a WP tag (Chief's exact glyphs unknown, verify in Chief). | Flags on devices | 2026-10-09 |
| EL6 | Device options (E-32, E-33, E-39): mounting, recess, size, height-to and Automatically Change Switch Type When Wiring live in `ElectricalLayer::options`, a table by device id written only for devices that differ from the defaults, not on `Device`: `Device` is built by literal in five crates. A copy or paste of a device does not carry its options (integration queue). | Fields on Device | 2026-10-09 |
| EL7 | Electrical Connection splines (E-37): a connection keeps its `from`, `to` and `arc_bulge`; `vertices` (empty = the arc, which is the two segments through the middle vertex of manual p. 696) hold the interior vertices and the curve is a Catmull-Rom spline through the ends and vertices. An unattached end is id 0 with its point in `from_at` / `to_at`. Curvature ratio = sagitta over chord, 0.2 by default, at most 0.5; the old 6 to 48 in clamp is gone. Dragging the middle handle of an arc bends it; any other edit makes it a spline; Reset Curvature clears the vertices and keeps the sign of `arc_bulge` as the original direction. A drag between two objects that are not a switch-to-load pair draws a plain spline (no control). Chief ties splines to cabinets and non-electrical symbols too: not built. | Arcs only | 2026-10-09 |
| EL8 | Rope lights (E-22, E-40): a rope light is a `RopeLightPath` in `ElectricalLayer::ropes` (points, closed, `RopeSpec`), not a device; `DeviceKind::RopeLight` still reads and draws as a two-point strip. A drag draws a two-point path (a click an 8 ft strip along +X), edited like an open polyline; a path starting under a wall cabinet takes the cabinet's bottom as its height. Lights are `Distance Between Lights` apart along the path, centered or from the start (a loop shares its length evenly). Schedule rows use entry ids from 2^40 so they never meet a device id; the Materials List adds an `Electrical / Rope Light` row in `lf`. The tray ceiling runs are computed (`tray_rope_lights`), not stored. | Strip device | 2026-10-09 |
| EL9 | Electrical defaults UI (E-19, E-21, E-22): one dialog with three tabs (Electrical Defaults, Electrical Connection, Rope Light), opened by the single Electrical leaf of Default Settings and by double-clicking an Electrical Tools button (the toolbar flyout calls `request_open_for`). Chief has three separate dialogs (manual p. 692). | Separate dialogs | 2026-10-09 |

The Select tool does not select splines or rope lights or draw the diamond handle. | None | 2026-10-09 |

| OM1 | Defaults per type (DW-124, DW-154, manual pp. 103, 571, 603): `OpeningVariantDefaults::types` holds one `TypeDefault` (a whole `Opening`, so the Defaults dialog is the Specification) per `DefaultKey` (kind, style, and Interior or Exterior for Hinged and Sliding doors only). The plain Hinged Door and the Window keep taking their size from `defaults.interior_door`, `exterior_door` and `window` (the code minimums and the older dialogs write those); every other key places its own size. The main window default (key Window/Window) is the one a window set to Use Default for its type follows; the Casement, Awning and other window tools have their own keys. The Default Settings tree has no leaf per type and the tool buttons do not open them on a double-click: Set as Default and the existing three dialogs write them (open, integration queue). Verify in Chief. | One slot per type | 2026-10-09 |
| OM2 | Dynamic defaults for doors and windows (manual p. 103): `OpeningSpec::dynamic` (`UseDefault`) holds a flag per group (Type, Casing, Lintel, Sash, Frame, Jamb, Hardware, Treatments, Framing, Rough Opening, Materials; the groups that apply differ by kind, `DynGroup::of_kind`). The tools turn them all on when they place from the defaults; an opening from an old plan has them off. A group the user edits in the opening's dialog is released unless its Use Default box was touched; ticking the box again makes the opening follow at the next `follow_defaults` (run by the tools, Set as Default and the integration hook), because the dialog cannot read the defaults. Set as Default copies the opening into the defaults and puts it back on Use Default. | Release on edit | 2026-10-09 |
| OM3 | Make Mulled Unit (DW-160, manual pp. 609 to 611) replaces the older Mull: it does not move anything, takes up to 24 in side to side or top to bottom, refuses a mixture of the two directions (block each row or column and mull the blocks, which makes a complex unit), and refuses bay, box and bow windows, niches and shaped windows with slanted sides. The components stay separate openings sharing `mull_group`; each carries the same `MulledSpec`, and the copy with the highest `revision` is the unit's (`Project::sync_unit_specs`), so the Mulled Unit panel can sit on any component's Options panel. `Project::mull_openings` (which closes gaps) remains for the transom. Chief opens a Mulled Unit Specification for the unit and a Window Specification for a component picked with Select Next Object; here one dialog does both. Single Wall Hole is stored and `Project::single_hole_boxes` gives the box, but the wall builder does not use it yet (integration queue); the mullion posts between components are built. | One dialog | 2026-10-09 |
| OM4 | Window Levels (DW-161, manual p. 611) are a number 0 to 9 on the opening (`OpeningSpec::level`). Level 0 draws in the layer colour, the others light grey; `mull::pick_order` is the order a click picks (level 0 first, then by level and sill). The Select tool's own pick and the Tab key do not use it yet (integration queue); the Select Next Object button does. | Grey for level > 0 | 2026-10-09 |
| OM5 | Bay, box and bow windows (DW-48, DW-155, manual pp. 604, 605, 634 to 642): the unit is one opening whose width is the opening in the wall; its `BayUnit` holds the Bay Angle (45, box 90), depth (0 takes the style's: bay 12 in, box 18 in, bow 11 1/2 in), the bow's sections (2 to 20), wall type, lowered ceiling, raised floor, roof, dimension, component and recess choices. New units are 50 in (bay and box) and 70 in (bow) wide, as the manual says; the older fixed 18 in projection and 96 in widths are gone. Component windows leave a 1 1/2 in trimmer at each end of a section unless No Trimmers or No Framing Between says otherwise. The unit's floor and ceiling are the room's unless raised or lowered; a unit on the first floor that is not raised gets a foundation prism 24 in deep in 3D and a footprint from `Project::bay_foundations` for the foundation builder. Explode makes one wall per section (the main wall's thickness, exterior side outward), one component window in each, a pass-through in the main wall, and a room named Bay with Raised Floor For Bump Out when the floor was raised or the ceiling lowered; the new walls cannot be marked Treat As Part Of Bay (no wall flag exists; integration queue). Verify in Chief. | Manual sizes | 2026-10-09 |
| OM6 | Header lines and opening display (DW-134, manual p. 612): with an Opening Header Lines layer present and on, a dashed line stands for each header; the layer is not in the default layer set (layers owner). Opening indicators in Vector Views with the hinge-side preference, Opaque Window/Door Glass, wrapped casing for doorways and custom muntins from CAD blocks are not built. | Not built | 2026-10-09 |
| DS12 | Saved Defaults and Default Sets (round 16, brief 26, manual pp. 104 to 110): the lists live in `Project::saved_defaults` (so they travel with the plan file and import with it); the values a tool reads stay where the tools read them: Text, Rich Text, Callouts, Markers and Notes in `Project::annot_defaults`, Manual Dimensions in `PlanDefaults::dimension_sets` (the older store), Revision Clouds, Arrows, Room Functions, Structural Member Reporting and Framing Types as page values in `PlanDefaults::pages`. Switching commits the live values into the saved default being left and loads the other; it is not an undo step. Edit in the Saved Defaults dialog makes the picked default the active one and opens the kind's own defaults dialog (Chief's Edit opens the picked one without activating it; the tools read the active values, so activating is how the dialog edits them). The dialog works on a snapshot and Cancel restores it. Notes join the annotation kinds a Default Set holds (the manual lists them); Room Functions, Structural Member Reporting and Framing Types have saved defaults but no Default Set slot. Their three small pages follow the manual's field names only (verify in Chief). | Everything in PlanDefaults, shared by all plans | 2026-10-09 |
| DS13 | Template decode names (brief 26): the Chief decode reads names, not the values behind them (layer sets, saved plan views, Rich Text defaults). A layer set arrives as a copy of the active one, a Rich Text default as the current Rich Text defaults, a plan view on the layer set of the same stem ("Plot Plan View" on "Plot Plan Layer Set"). A Default Set is made for every name that has both a dimension set and a Rich Text default ("1/4-inch Scale", "Plot Plan", "NKBA", ...), with the layer set of that stem when there is one; this pairing is our guess at how Daniel's template groups them (verify in Chief). The dozen starter views (`STARTER_VIEWS`) are the guide's and Daniel's working views; the Residential template's own twelve names are not decoded. | Nothing seeded beyond the counts | 2026-10-09 |
| DS14 | Dynamic defaults (brief 26, manual p. 103): a group of settings follows its default when its state is recorded as Use Default (`FollowTable`, a slot of `SavedDefaults`) or, when nothing is recorded, while its value is still the old default's (the rule cabinets already used). Doors and windows keep their older per-opening flags. One watcher (`plan_defaults::track`, called each frame from the defaults windows' host) compares the plan defaults with the last frame and, on a change, lets walls (type and thickness, height), doors, windows and cabinets follow as one undo step "Default Settings", so no dialog needs a hook. The Use Default state is not yet set by the wall dialog (a wall edited in its dialog keeps its value, which no longer equals the old default, so it does not follow). Rooms, stairs, roofs and electrical device heights are not wired (queued). | A flag on every object kind | 2026-10-09 |

Cabinets, construction lines and electrical devices keep their own buttons. After it, objects on the default follow (DS14). The terrain and its paths are refused by name. | One generic button | 2026-10-09 |
| DS16 | Reverse Plan (brief 26, supersedes the exception in DS7): objects on hidden and locked layers are mirrored with the rest, because Chief flips the whole plan; the layers are opened for the turn and put back, and the step is still one undo step. | The old exception | 2026-10-09 |
| DS17 | Rotate Plan View (brief 26, supersedes the open part of DS8): Tools > Rotate Plan View asks for an angle in degrees and sets the view to it (90 twice is 90), shown from -180 to 180 (270 reads -90). A positive angle turns the plan counterclockwise, like the 90 Degrees Left row (Chief's sign is not confirmed: verify in Chief). A saved view that has Remember Zoom/Rotation (on by default here) stores the rotation shown when it is saved or left and brings it back when it is shown; other views open north up. The CAD Detail from View and Plan Footprint tools do not inherit the rotation yet (queued). | A rotation on every window | 2026-10-09 |
