Every view of a plan is saved, so Saved is shown checked and cannot be cleared. Show Watermark and Poché stay where View > Watermark and View > Poché keep them (`print_setup.watermark.on`, `styles.poche`) and follow a rename. Never Save keeps the camera, defaults and rotation from being captured when the view is left; Save Plan View still stores them. Prompt to Save has no prompt yet (there is no close-view step to hang it on; queued). The pony wall options are not built. | A second copy of the switches in the view | 2026-10-09 |
| DS19 | Templates (brief 26, manual pp. 110 to 114): a Plan Studio plan template is a JSON file (`.pstemplate`) holding a purged copy of the plan and its defaults, kept in `~/.plan-studio/templates/plans`; metric templates carry " - Metric" like Chief's. Save as Template lists the data categories to delete (`PurgeCategory`; our list, verify in Chief), can make the template the default for the units of the plan, and can open a new plan from it. File > New Plan uses the default template of its units; when that file is gone a prompt offers Load Installed or Load Custom. The chooser hides metric or U.S. templates on request. Chief's own `.plan` templates keep seeding the defaults as before. CAD Details are kept by a purge unless chosen. | Reuse the Chief template path | 2026-10-09 |
| DS20 | Import Settings (brief 26, manual pp. 114 to 118): categories Multiple Saved Defaults (per kind, and the Default Sets), Default Settings (walls, doors, windows, cabinets, rooms, roofs, text pages, schedules, electrical, materials), Layer Sets, Note Types, Saved Plan Views and Wall Types; CAD, Floor, Framing, Foundation and General Plan defaults are never offered. Sources: Plan Studio plans, plan templates, defaults files and Chief plans and layouts (the decoded names and values); a layout offers Default Sets and Layer Sets only. Settings only import from files in the same units. Rename Imported adds the number the manual says (" 2", then " 3"). The legacy `.layers`, `.cadefs`, wall `.dat` and note type `.json` imports read Plan Studio's JSON exchange files; Chief's binary files cannot be read (a `.layers` file is read for its layer set names when the container decodes). | Only layer sets import | 2026-10-09 |
| DS21 | Edit Active Default on Double-Click (brief 26, manual pp. 105, 133): on by default; off makes a double-click on a Manual Dimensions, Text, Rich Text, Callout, Marker, Note, Arrow or Revision Cloud button open its Saved Defaults dialog. The switch is in the Saved Defaults dialog and kept for the session until the Preferences General panel (PR-22) takes it over. | A Preferences row | 2026-10-09 |
| FF1 | Stem wall height (brief 17, R-61, R-127; supersedes 301): the Stem Wall Height of Build Foundation and the Foundation Defaults runs from the top of the footing to the underside of Floor 1's platform, which bears on top of the wall; Hang 1st Floor Platform Inside Foundation Walls builds the wall up to the top of the platform instead. The model's `StemWall { height }` stays the distance from the bottom of the foundation to Floor 1's finished level (Floor 0 sits that far below), so the dialog adds the platform (`FoundationSpec::to_kind`) and the walls are `height` less the platform tall, or the whole `height` when the platform hangs. Grade beams carry the platform on top too (Floor 0 is a platform taller than pier plus beam). The Basement Ceiling Height shown is the stem wall height less the slab, as the manual says. | Bearing platform; hang is an option | 2026-10-09 |
| FF2 | Basement tiers (brief 17, R-18, R-139; refines 300): Build Foundation > Walls with Footings names the room it makes by its clear height under the platform: 72 in or more after a 4 in slab is a finished Basement (slab floor, ceiling), 48 in or more is a Basement without a floor or ceiling finish that counts toward the Living Area (the 48 in rule of `living::room_in_living_area`, DECISIONS 300 stands for the type), less is a Crawl Space. The dialog's Auto choice follows this; Basement and Crawl Space force the type (a forced Basement still has no slab below 72 in). | Three tiers | 2026-10-09 |
| FF3 | Garage and Slab rooms under a foundation (brief 17, R-133, R-135): a Garage or Slab room (by its Room Type's function, or the Monolithic Slab Foundation flag) whose floor height is still 0 is lowered when the foundation is built: by the floor platform plus Garage Floor to Stem Wall Top (12 in) under Walls with Footings or Grade Beams on Piers, by Lower Garage Floor (3 1/2 in) under a Monolithic Slab; a floor height the user or the type already set (the Garage type starts at -24 in) is kept. The garage gets a slab of its own on Floor 0, stem walls whose top is the slab 12 in up (never shorter than Minimum Garage Height, 24 in), a Slab room under it that supplies the floor above, and Floor Supplied by the Foundation Room Below ticked on the Floor 1 room. Under a monolithic slab every room is a slab room, the curbs between garage and house stand at the house floor height and each room has its own slab. The Build Foundation box 'Build Garage Floor' is `FoundationSettings::garage_floor`; off treats garages like other rooms. | Lower by platform plus 12 in, or 3 1/2 in | 2026-10-09 |
| FF4 | Which walls get a foundation (brief 17, R-135): every exterior wall that is not invisible or a railing and bounds a room that builds a foundation below (a room with Build Foundation Below off leaves its walls out; walls that bound no room keep the older rule: all exterior walls); an interior wall, railing or invisible wall with Create Wall/Footing Below (`WallFoundation::create_below`; the Wall Specification check box is a hook in docs/integration-queue.md); a bearing wall; a wall between a slab room and the rest; a wall between rooms of different floor heights or stem wall heights. | As the manual lists | 2026-10-09 |
| FF5 | Stepped foundation (brief 17, R-138, R-140): each wall's top is the higher and its bottom the lower of the rooms it bounds (floor height less the platform, or the room's own Stem Wall Height; no lower than Minimum Height); the footings stay level at Floor 0's base unless a minimum or a room's own height reaches deeper, and Floor 0 then goes down with the deepest wall. Steps are found where foundation walls meet at different tops (`foundation::step_markers`, a step is 1/16 in or more) and an S is drawn there on the 'Footings, Step Markers' layer when Show S Markers is on. 'Stepped stem walls have vertical footings' (`FoundationSettings::vertical_step_footings`) gives the taller wall at a step a vertical footing; a monolithic slab's edge walls get the chamfer width and height (4 in) of the Foundation Defaults. | Steps found from wall tops | 2026-10-09 |
| FF6 | Garage cutouts (brief 17, R-135): each door in a wall that bounds a Garage or Slab room leaves a cutout in the stem wall or curb under it, as an opening of Floor 0's foundation wall: as wide as the rough opening plus Add for Concrete Cutout on each side (`foundation::garage_cut_width`), the whole height of the wall, drawn as a Doorway. The condition that the wall's Main Layer be Concrete or Brick is not checked (the foundation wall type is concrete); Show In Floor Below of the Rough Opening panel is kept with the door but the cutout always shows on Floor 0. | Every door in a garage wall | 2026-10-09 |
| FF7 | Auto Rebuild Foundation (brief 17, R-130; manual p. 745): `FoundationSettings::auto_rebuild` is stored with the build (`FloorSettings::foundation_options`); `foundation_signature` digests what the build reads from Floor 1 (walls, flags, rooms' types, heights and stem wall heights, doors, fireplaces, the platform) and `Project::auto_rebuild_foundation` builds again when it changed. `foundation_view::frame` runs it once a frame from `PlanApp::update` and, like Auto Rebuild Roofs, it is not an undo step of its own. Floor 0 cannot be deleted while it is on (`delete_floor`, `rooms_edit::delete_foundation`), and the foundation tools warn instead of drawing, moving or deleting on Floor 0; the Wall tools and the Room Specification on Floor 0 are hooks in docs/integration-queue.md. | Follows the edit, no step of its own | 2026-10-09 |
| FF8 | Foundation Defaults values chosen (brief 17, R-129..R-134), not captured from Chief: Minimum Height 12 in; Garage Floor to Stem Wall Top 12 in, Lower Garage Floor 3 1/2 in and Minimum Garage Height 24 in (the manual's figures); pier width 12 in, Round; rebar 1/2 in (4/8) with 40 diameters of lap, 2 bars in the footing, 1 bar per course every 32 in in walls, 4 in a pier, 1 bar every 18 in in a slab; Use Mesh on; Foam Seal and Termite Flashing off; S markers on; Auto Rebuild and Hang Platform off. The panels are the Foundation Defaults (Edit > Default Settings > Foundation > Foundation, `Action::FoundationDefaults`) and the same body in Build Foundation, which also makes its choices the new defaults. The Edit Default Slab Footing, Edit Default Foundation Wall and Edit Garage Curb buttons all open the Wall Type Definitions on the plan's one foundation wall type (Plan Studio has a single foundation wall default). | One foundation wall type for the three Edit buttons | 2026-10-09 |
| FF9 | Piers (brief 17, R-132): Depth is the pier's height (`FoundationOptions::pier_height`), Maximum Separation the spacing (`pier_positions` splits each wall into equal spans no longer than it, with a pier at both ends), Width the diameter of a Round Pier or the side of a Square Pad (a pad is as thick as the depth). The Pier/Pad Specification turns one into the other with its Type choice, keeping width, top and depth. A pier or pad is still free to stand anywhere, not only under a wall (R-142). | Round by default | 2026-10-09 |
| FF10 | Options panel (brief 17, R-134): the rebar, mesh, Foam Seal and Termite Flashing choices are stored but never drawn; `foundation::foundation_takeoff(project)` turns them into Materials List rows (footing, wall horizontal, wall vertical, pier and slab rebar in feet with the lap, slab mesh in square feet, foam seal and termite flashing in linear feet), for the Materials List owner (integration queue). | Rows from one function | 2026-10-09 |
| FF11 | Terrain under a foundation (brief 17, R-140): `foundation::terrain_elevation` puts the automatic terrain 6 in below the tops of the stem walls or grade beams and 8 in below the top of a monolithic slab; Terrain > Building Pad uses it when the pad's elevation is Automatic (`site_view::auto_building_pad` sets the pad's first-floor figure so the pad lands there). Daylight, walkout and stepped terrain conditions stay hand set in the Terrain Specification, as the manual says. | Automatic only | 2026-10-09 |
| FF12 | Build New Floor options (brief 17, R-119..R-121): Move Highest Floor's Roof Up moves the roof records of the highest floor to the new floor and raises their vertices by the floor-to-floor rise (Insert New Floor Below keeps them on their floor and raises them), only when roof planes are built there and neither the Auto Rebuild Roofs preference nor that roof's own switch is on (`RoofChoice`). Step floor/ceiling elevations: building above, each room of the floor below whose ceiling (floor height plus ceiling height) is not the default gives the room above it the same rise as floor height, taking the tallest where one room is over several; inserting below, each room of the floor above gives the new room under it a ceiling height up to its floor height. Without the step option, adding a floor above resets the ceiling heights of rooms whose floors are raised or lowered (manual p. 772). Insert New Floor Below opens the Build New Floor dialog on Place Below as 'Insert New Floor' (the empty instant version stays in Insert New Floor); Build New Floor opens the Floor Defaults of the new floor next. At most 30 living floors (the foundation and the attic are not counted). | Rise by room | 2026-10-09 |
| FF13 | Attic floor (brief 17, R-126; supersedes the Attic room of 309): rooms cannot be created on the Attic floor, so Build Roof's attic refresh makes only the attic walls and clears name entries from the Attic floor; `Project::attic_floor_warning` and `foundation_view::attic_warning` show the manual's warning in the status line once per visit when walls other than the attic walls, or other objects, are on it. | Status line, once | 2026-10-09 |
| FF14 | Fireplaces built into a wall (brief 17, CB-87, CB-500..CB-502; supersedes the position of 202): the back of the fireplace is flush with the outside face of the wall and the front stands out into the room by what the body is deeper than the wall is thick; on an exterior wall it always faces the interior whichever side is clicked (when a room beside the wall says which side that is), on an interior wall the edge clicked. The Depth handle of a fireplace built in (`fireplace_view::slide_in_wall`, hooked into `tools::library::apply_drag`) slides it toward the outside until its front is flush with the inside edge of the wall. The Rough Opening panel (the door's) widens the wall cut by its extra width each side and draws dashed with Show Rough Opening in Plan. The Firebox panel has No Firebox and an Offset to the left or right; the General panel has Hearth Depth and Height and Suppress Dimensions; the plan shows the width and the firebox width dimensions beside the fireplace. Drawing the fireplace moved from `roof_view::draw_roofs` to `render::draw_plan` after the walls. | Manual position | 2026-10-09 |
| FF15 | Fireplace foundation (brief 17, CB-500): building Walls with Footings or Grade Beams on Piers puts a block on Floor 0 under every masonry fireplace on Floor 1 (same footprint, position in a wall and material, No Firebox, no hearth, mantel or chimney; `Fireplace::base_of` names the fireplace above) that runs from the footing to the underside of the platform; the next build replaces the blocks. A copied or pasted fireplace carries its specification (`Floor::symbol_for_copy` writes the record into the symbol's options and `Project::add_symbol` reads it back for the new id). | Block per masonry fireplace | 2026-10-09 |
| FF16 | Slab and Pier/Pad specification (brief 17, R-85, R-141): the Slab Specification has Hole in Slab (the slab becomes a slab hole and must lie inside another slab), Elevation Reference (the six of the manual, resolved at the slab's middle; the slab stays stored floor-relative and `Slab::elevation_base` only says what Top and Bottom are shown from), Top and Bottom (editing the bottom changes the thickness), Has Footing with Height, Width and Footing Offset (how far the footing reaches past the slab's edges, drawn in 3D). Components and the other common panels come from the shell. | Display only reference | 2026-10-09 |

### Round 16: text macros, Find/Replace and the energy export (brief 27)
| # | Decision | Assumption in code until decided | Raised |
|---|---|---|---|
| TM1 | Ruby macros are out of scope (manual pp. 569, 1446, 1456): a user macro is plain text that may use other `%macros%` (nested up to six deep, a loop is reported). The Text Macro Management window has Edit, New, Copy, Delete, Import, Export and Show Evaluation Error but no Migrate and no macros kept with library objects. | Plain text macros | 2026-10-09 |
| TM2 | Macro names (the manual names only `%date.short%`, so the rest are ours, verify in Chief): global `plan.name`, `plan.date`, `floor`, `floor.number`, `floor.count`, `floor.height`; room `room.name`, `room.nvp_name`, `room.number`, `room.type`, `room.area`, `room.perimeter`, `room.ceiling_height`; time `date.short` (M/D/YYYY), `date.long`, `date.iso`, `date.year`, `date.month`, `date.day`, `date.weekday`, `time.short`, `time.long`, all UTC (no time zone is available); characters `char.degree`, `char.plusminus`, `char.diameter`, `char.feet`, `char.inches`, `char.bullet`, `char.copyright`, `char.registered`, `char.trademark`, `char.half`, `char.quarter`, `char.threequarters`, `char.newline`, `char.percent`; Project Information `<owner>.<name>` in lower case with spaces as `_`, plus the older title-block names (`%client%`, `%address%`, `%designer%`...). The referenced-object names are the tutorial's (`%comment%`, `%description%`, `%automatic_description%`, `%automatic_label%`, `%nominal_size%`...). A built-in name cannot be a user macro (`plan_core::macros::is_reserved`). | As listed | 2026-10-09 |
| TM3 | Live texts: a Text or Rich Text with a macro keeps what was typed in `Project::macro_texts` (by the text's id; skipped when empty) and its CAD item holds the evaluated text, refreshed by `Project::sync_macro_texts` from `sync_annotations`, so the editor's refresh keeps it current. A text whose item no longer equals the last evaluation was edited by something else (spell check, a painter): the entry is dropped and the edit stands. Copies, pastes and imports are plain text. Callouts, markers and notes still expand global and user macros once, when their dialog is OK'd; their own annotation macros stay live as before. Replaces the one-time expansion of round 15. | As described | 2026-10-09 |
| TM4 | Project Information owners (manual pp. 569, 570): Project, Designer, Client are fixed. Their system names are Project Number, Address, Lot, Date, Revision; Designer Name, Company, Drawn By, Checked By; Client Name, Address, Phone, Email (Chief's names are not captured). They read and write `ProjectInfo`, except Lot, which is stored. Custom owners and the custom names of Designer and Client are JSON under the reserved key `plan_core::macros::OWNERS_KEY` of `ProjectInfo::custom`, so the plan needs no field of its own and every path that stores `ProjectInfo` keeps them; the custom names of the Project owner are `ProjectInfo::custom` itself, which layouts already read as `%custom.name%`. The dialog replaces the Client, Project, Designer and Custom Fields tabs with Owners and Revisions. | As described | 2026-10-09 |
| TM5 | Wall R-values for the envelope export: wall layers carry no R-value yet, so the cavity value is 3.5 per inch (5 for foam) of an insulation layer found by name or material, cavity when it is the main layer and continuous otherwise, and `Options::default_wall_cavity_r` (0) otherwise. Floors and ceilings sum the R-values of the layered definitions (`plan_core::assemblies`). A first-floor room with a monolithic slab is a slab on grade; any other first-floor room is a floor over unconditioned space. Wall area is centerline length times height; a wall is on the envelope when exactly one side lies in a conditioned room (a garage or porch wall is too). Fences, railings and deck walls are left out. | As described | 2026-10-09 |
| TM6 | The `.rxl` element names (`REScheck`, `Project`, `Envelope`, `Floors`, `Slabs`, `Ceilings`, `Walls`, `Doors`, `Windows`...) are our reading of the manual's description (pp. 1304 to 1306), not REScheck's schema, which is not in the manual: open an export in REScheck before relying on it. On Center spacing is always 16. Orientation of exactly 45 degrees goes to North, then South, then East (the manual's "45 degrees or less" for each quarter overlaps at the diagonals). Direction uses the terrain's North Pointer angle. | As described | 2026-10-09 |
| TM7 | Find/Replace Text: Searching In is Current View (the active floor), Current File (every floor), All Open Files (every floor and every layout file stored in the plan), Selected Objects. Macro names, not values, are searched. Expand Percent Signs ignores a run of one or two `%` outside a macro. Replace All is one undo step across the plan and the layout files. Show in File goes to the floor of a plan result; for a layout result it posts `FindReplaceDialog::take_goto` for the shell. The Highlight Color is stored; the selection highlight is not recoloured. Generated callout text is not searched, its record is. | As described | 2026-10-09 |
| TM8 | Word-processor font sizing (manual p. 523) is a stored flag (`TextBox::word_sizing`); the plan, layouts and PDF draw both sizings at the CAD size. Paragraph Options are per text object (`TextBox::line_spacing`, `para_left`, `para_right`, `para_indent`), not per paragraph. Tab columns are `\t` characters in the text, laid out by `layout_runs` (a column as wide as its widest cell, or `TextBox::tab_width`), so they draw everywhere a box draws; Convert to Text replaces them by spaces. | As described | 2026-10-09 |
| TM9 | Replace Fonts: the window lists the fonts of the text styles, rich text runs and live texts, marks the ones with no installed family as Missing (a family matches by `fonts::family_candidates`), and replaces family and face (Regular, Bold, Italic, Bold Italic) in one undo step. The Edit menu line no longer opens the Text Styles list (the `edit.replace_fonts` constant stays for `main.rs`). | As described | 2026-10-09 |
| DM15 | Angular dimension test points (round 15 finish): the tool's third click angle-snaps from the second and rounds its length to the grid, so a click that is not a whole grid length from the previous one gives an angle a little off a right angle. The unit test clicks (0,0), (96,0), (96,96) and expects 45.0 degrees. Chief's behaviour here is unconfirmed (verify in Chief). |
| FL1 | Build Framing is a command (round 16 brief 30; manual pp. 889, 914, 915; corrects the tabs of 112): its one page holds the Automatic Framing Defaults button, Automatically Rebuild Framing (Floor, Ceiling, Wall, Roof) and Build Framing Once (Floor and Ceiling with a floor choice, Wall, Roof). The panels moved to the Automatic Framing Defaults view of the same window (Floor Levels, Wall, Openings, Roof, Trusses, Posts); the button switches to it, OK or Cancel returns to the command. Brief 29's dialog replaces this stand-in; Default Settings > Framing opens the same panels. | The stand-in panels are in `dialogs/framing.rs` | 2026-10-09 |
| FL2 | Build Framing Once floor choice (CB-638): `BuildOptions::floor_pick` and `ceiling_pick` are Current Floor (the menu item's floor, or every floor for Build All Framing; Plan Studio's own entry), All Floors or one floor. Wall and Roof follow the menu item. The checked parts are the saved Build flags. The per-group Retain check boxes stay on the command page though Chief has none (Retain Framing is per object there); they keep older plans working. | A Floor selector with only All Floors and the floors | 2026-10-09 |
| FL3 | The framing detail options Chief puts in the Automatic Framing Defaults panels but `FramingDefaults` lacks (lap or butt, blocking style, rim joist width, Stagger or Flush, corner and intersection styles, plate connection, Stagger Blocking, mitre options, header maximum depth, List Cut Header Lengths, Wall Detail from exterior, cross boxes) live in `BuildOptions::detail` because `defaults.rs` belongs to brief 29. The roof ones (lookouts, Trim To Soffits, Overframing, hip girder, rake overhang) are in `RoofFramingDefaults`. Defaults keep older results: lookouts off, hip girder count 1, header maximum depth 0, Trim To Soffits off (Chief's own defaults are not captured; verify in Chief). | Fields in FramingDefaults | 2026-10-09 |
| FL4 | Joists over a support that exists (a Bearing Wall, or a beam marked Bearing Beam): Lap puts 8 in of overlap centred on the support, the second joist standing one thickness over toward the middle of the platform; Butt meets on the centre line. A beam whose top is 1 in or more above the joist bottoms holds them by its sides (no lap, no new beam). A drawn Bearing Line keeps its older behaviour: a gap and a new 4x10 beam. Bearing Walls only matter when only the exterior walls bear (`BearingMode::ExteriorAndBearingLines`); joists then run across the longest bearing wall or beam. Bearing walls get a double top plate and at least a double header. | Lap and butt for Bearing Lines too | 2026-10-09 |
| FL5 | Framing Reference (CB-220, CB-233, CB-263, CB-264): each floor uses its own first Framing Reference Marker, else the first one on the first floor; a marker anywhere on the floor anchors walls, joists, ceiling joists and rafters (the earlier 24 in reach is gone). `BuildOptions::floor_reference` switches it per floor (on by default) and `roof_reference` for rafters. Walls have no Use Framing Reference box yet, so they follow the floor switch. Move to Framing Ref moves a parallel group of manual members by the offset that puts the first one on the grid (spacing: the framing defaults for its kind). Deck joists take the marker through `build_deck_framing_ref` (the deck builder has to pass it). | Marker within 24 in of a wall | 2026-10-09 |
| FL6 | Framing Groups (CB-256): only when only the exterior walls bear, a wall with rooms of different groups on its two sides ends the platform. Build Framing for Selected Object on a room asks "new Framing Group?" only when another room of its group is in the same platform: Yes gives it the next group number, No builds across the platform as it is. In the all-walls mode every room is a platform of its own, so nothing is asked. Retain Floor/Ceiling Framing on a room protects the old joists inside it and builds none there. | Always ask | 2026-10-09 |
| FL7 | Wall Details (CB-645, manual p. 924) are CAD detail floors (`DetailSource::WallDetail { wall }`): one is made for every wall with built framing when framing is built, named from the wall's label (the label the user typed, else `W<n>`, n counting from 1 among the floor's walls), redrawn from the members on every build and member edit, dropped with its wall if the user drew nothing in it. Members are closed polylines on `Detail, Framing`, titles and dimensions on `Detail, Notes` and `Detail, Lines`; the program remembers what it drew (`DetailMap`), so notes, dimensions and CAD the user adds stay. The Project Browser lists them in a Wall Details node, "Floor - label" when there are several floors (not one folder per floor). Member edits are buttons on the selected box: Build Framing for Parent Object(s), Find Wall, Flat to Inside or Outside, Delete Framing Member(s); a rebuild replaces them unless Retain Wall Framing is on. Seen from the exterior by default (Build Wall Framing Details from Exterior). | A dedicated Wall Detail window | 2026-10-09 |
| FL8 | The Truss Detail (CB-311, manual p. 943) is a CAD detail floor (`DetailSource::TrussDetail`), made when the first truss exists and kept current by every truss edit, build, move and delete: one diagram per configuration with its label, and the quantity in parentheses when several share it. It is not linked back to the model the way Chief's is: Find Trusses (from a selected diagram) and Open Truss Detail (from a selected truss) go between them, and deleting a drawing deletes nothing in the plan. A Truss Detail window (Build > Framing) lists the same with Find Trusses, Open Truss Detail and Force Truss Rebuild. | Truss Detail linked to the trusses | 2026-10-09 |
| FL9 | Truss labels (CB-302, manual p. 944): `TR-n` for roof and girder trusses and `FTR-n` for floor and ceiling trusses, numbered by first appearance over every floor (manual and laid-out trusses in floor and creation order, then the trusses the roof framing made). Two trusses share a label when their specs are equal with the span rounded to 1/16 in and the plies equal; a girder with other plies is another configuration. A label the user typed in the Label panel wins. | Label by member id | 2026-10-09 |
| FL10 | Manual contradiction (pp. 940, 959): Calculate Chords/Webbing in Materials List is described both as counting the truss as one object and as counting the chord and web pieces. Plan Studio reads the box as its name says: checked counts the pieces (the default, which is what the takeoff always did), unchecked counts the truss as one object (field `TrussSpec::calc_chords`; the takeoff reading it is open). | Checked counts one object | 2026-10-09 |
| FL11 | Maximum Horizontal Span (CB-326): a smaller maximum refines the webbing of the chosen type along King post, Fink, Howe, Double Fink, Double Howe (the first whose longest panel fits); it never coarsens one, and Scissor, Attic and Mono keep theirs. Double Fink and Double Howe geometry is Plan Studio's own (verify in Chief). Reduced Gable has no overhang and drops the apex by a chord depth, Drop Hip by the rafter depth (7 1/4 in), Energy Heel raises the heel at least 7 in, End Truss puts verticals at the stud spacing, Sloping Flat Truss has parallel chords a Structure Depth apart. | Truss families by name only | 2026-10-09 |
| FL12 | Some options are data and display only so far: Trim Framing To Soffits marks the rafter labels (depth trim is not in the 3D profile), End Profile shapes are stored and listed but the 3D box stays square, Rotate Flat to Inside or Outside turns a wall member in a Wall Detail and a General Framing member by 90 degrees, but aligns with the layer's face only for wall members. Roof Overframing puts a shoe plate (a Ledger member) under the eave of a plane built over another; the plane's rafters are not changed. | Not built | 2026-10-09 |
| FL13 | Posts under beams (CB-262): Build Framing puts a post (a built member, wall group) where a Floor/Ceiling Beam of the floor above, or a ceiling beam of the floor, crosses one of its walls, and leaves out the wall's studs that it stands among. Top plates are not cut around it. | Cut the plates too | 2026-10-09 |
| FL14 | Tray ceiling framing (the hook of brief 16): the ceiling group builds `frame_tray_ceiling` for every tray without a Caution and without Retain Framing; its members carry `TRAY_MEMBER_FLAG | tray id` in `wall_id`, which makes them Ceiling-group members though they are plates and studs, and lets Build Framing for Selected Object replace one tray's members. | Wall group | 2026-10-09 |
| FL15 | Start and End Indicators (manual p. 925): Preferences has no such switch, so a selected manual line member always shows its S and E (`framing_view::set_start_end_indicators` is the hook for the Preferences owner). | A Preferences page | 2026-10-09 |
| FL16 | Edits outside the files of brief 30, each small: `editor/dispatch.rs` (framing buttons and commands), `tools/mod.rs` (host_frame call), `shell/docks.rs` (Wall Details node), `shell/spec_dialogs.rs` (direction lines and laid-out trusses open their Specification, OK calls `FramingMemberDialog::apply`), `dialogs.rs` (two mod lines), `dialogs/defaults.rs` untouched, `plan-core details/cad_detail.rs` (two `DetailSource` variants) and `dialogs/details/management.rs` (their labels), `tools/details/cad_detail.rs` (cut blocking is a box with one diagonal), `plan-framing lib.rs` (re-exports). | Queue the hooks only | 2026-10-09 |
| DT1 | Defaults follow Daniel's working template (x17 Working Template 2025-08-20), not Chief's Residential Template. Decided by Daniel on 2026-10-09 after the manual audit's defaults comparison (docs/chief-manual-coverage/defaults-that-differ.md): ceiling height, window size, roof pitch, wall types (108), layer sets (34), dimension sets (14), text sets (13) and saved views (20) come from his template; Chief's Residential Template values are reference only. Where his template has no value, Chief's default applies. The embedded template (crates/plan-app/assets/templates/chief-x18-daniel.json) is the source; brief 26 (saved defaults and views) and brief 12 (wall types) fill the remaining gaps from the template file at run time. | Daniel | 2026-10-09 |
| DT2 | Daniel confirmed (2026-10-09) the four Chief-matching behaviours adopted after the manual audit: duplicate layout page labels are allowed; Alt is each tool's Alternate behaviour and Ctrl/Cmd overrides snaps and restrictions (brief 10 implements the swap, see decisions-corrections.md); exterior dimensions and the automatic roof rebuild when a room closes (Auto Rebuild on); sheet sizes are program-wide. Undo levels stay at 100 (Chief: 50, range 1-100) as a deliberate difference. | Daniel | 2026-10-09 |
| SC14 | Schedule numbering made consistent with Chief (manual p. 715; refines DECISIONS 44). Existing objects are numbered in ascending Label order and equal labels go by placement (ids ascend as objects are made), not by reading order; a schedule that lists nothing yet is a record too (`Schedule::numbers_recorded`, set by `record_numbers`), so objects placed after the schedule exists go below the last number in placement order and a deleted one leaves a gap until Renumber Schedule; only a schedule saved before Round 16 (no record, flag false) still numbers by position. An object left out (Include in Schedule off) has no number and takes no slot. Tests that expected reading order (two in `opening_edit`, the `s26_openings_r14` renumber scenario, the `opening_view` mark test) now expect placement order; the windows test creates its schedule after the windows so the gap it checks exists in the record. Limit: a number is recorded only when the schedule is created, renumbered or a row is moved, so an object placed after creation and then deleted does not leave a gap for the objects placed after it (they are renumbered by placement) until a hook records numbers on placement (integration-queue). | Placement order and recorded gaps | 2026-10-09 |
| QA-32 | Story pole marks: inclusion is a flag, not membership. `PoleSetup::marks` lists every `MarkKind` (eight entries, `MarkKind::ALL` order) and `PoleMark::included` says whether the pole locates it; the Locate check box writes only that flag, and the name and outer-string switches never add or remove a mark. A mark list saved before the flag (only located kinds) loads with the missing kinds added as not located, so a template reload no longer re-adds Chief's default marks after the user cleared them. Story pole code reads `PoleSetup::located` / `locates`. Un-ignored s47 `every_field_of_every_generic_page_round_trips_through_the_template`; core test `story_pole_marks_are_flags_that_survive_the_template_json`. | Round-trip of every generic page field | 2026-10-09 |
| DS22 | Use Default for rooms, stairs and roofs (brief 26, manual p. 103): not wired as a follower. A room has no stored heights of its own (they are the floor's, and Chief's Floor Defaults apply to floors made afterwards), and moving existing floors' ceilings when a default changes would shift every elevation above them. Stairs and roofs keep their values in models other briefs are rewriting (21, 18/19/20); their owners add a follower to `plan_defaults::follow_changed_defaults` with the group already listed in `DefaultKind::groups()`. Walls, doors, windows, cabinets and electrical heights follow. Marked "verify in Chief". | Chief's rule for a room's heights | 2026-10-09 |
| R16-12a | Wall types round 16 (brief 12): a layer's Fill is `WallLayerSpec::fill`, read by `Project::wall_layer_fill` after any Fill Style dialog assignment (`FillTarget::WallLayer`), so the existing plan renderers draw it without a second source. Roles in the dialog are Framing, Air Gap, Standard, Cladding and Finish (Sheathing stays loadable from layered assemblies). A Main layer must be next to another Main layer; the last Main layer cannot be unticked; Move Up and Down stay on for every row. Extension applies to Exterior layers only. | As built; verify in Chief | 2026-10-09 |
| R16-12b | Wall Type Definitions OK is one undo step ("Wall Type Definitions"); it writes the edited types to the defaults (so the template) and the plan, sets walls of an edited type to the type's new total thickness (centre kept, no re-solve of joins: the joins re-solve on the next edit), and removes the types Delete All Unused took from both. Delete All Unused keeps the type on screen and is off when the host cannot list the types in use (the Wall Specification's Define button: queued). | As built; verify in Chief | 2026-10-09 |
| R16-02a | Group drag of page CAD (layout window): a press on a page drawing that is part of a mixed selection (drawing plus boxes, or several drawings) drags the whole group as one "Move Layout Drawing" step. The drawing's eight resize handles are offered only when it is the sole selected object, because a line's bounds are flat and its mid-edge handles sat on its midpoint, which turned the press into a resize. Chief's own handle behaviour for mixed selections is unknown; verify in Chief. |
| RB15 | Roof Baseline Polyline tool ignores the centerline snap (OnObject): the baseline sits against the outside wall face, and a snap to the wall centerline would put a porch outline inside the wall. Other snaps (endpoint, intersection, grid) still apply. Hooks: `roof_view::rebuild` calls `gable_line::apply_stored` and `roof_trim::regenerate`. | Raw click point on a centerline snap | 2026-10-09 |
| FF17 | A foundation wall is cut where a wall of Floor 1 ends on it (brief 17 finish, R-138, R-140; refines FF5): Floor 1's long exterior wall that an interior wall meets in the middle is one wall, so a stepped foundation needs the two floor heights to meet at that point. `plan_walls` cuts a straight wall at each T-junction (an end of another wall within half the thickness plus 1 in of its centreline, at least 1 in from either end), plans each piece from the rooms beside it, and joins neighbouring pieces whose top, bottom, slab edge and Create Wall/Footing Below are alike, so a level plan keeps its long walls whole. A door's cutout goes in the piece the door stands in, at its distance from that piece's start. Curved walls are not cut. | Heights from the wall's midpoint only | 2026-10-09 |
| FF18 | Plan draw order of decks and level changes (brief 17 finish, CB-86, R-86): the decking boards and the dashed level-change lines draw in `render::draw_plan` right after the fireplaces, over the walls, no longer from `roof_view::draw_roofs` (which sat under the walls and returned early without roof planes). Same stroke and layer rules as before. | Drawn with the roofs under the walls | 2026-10-09 |
| FF19 | Scenario fixtures (brief 17 finish): the s92 house has one floor (a new project has no Attic floor), so Build New Floor gives two floors and Insert New Floor gives two; its partition runs to the centrelines of the front and back walls so it divides the house into two rooms; the new floor is read by index because undo then redo keeps the viewer on the floor that has the same name and height, not on the new one. The Move Roof Up test turns the Auto Rebuild Roofs preference off where it checks the roof's own switch (the test plan's preference default is on). | Counts and a one-in gap copied from an earlier draft | 2026-10-09 |
| EL12 | Auto Place Outlets by the room's electrical rules (brief 25 finish, manual p. 447; E-24): `place::auto_place_outlets_by_rules` takes the rules per room label. Exterior rooms, Porches and Open Below get none. Hybrid rooms (garage, slab) get `Fewer`: twice the spacing between outlets (24 ft), a garage still gets one GFCI per bay. A kitchen or bath with base cabinets on its walls gets GFCI outlets over them at the Above Base Cabinet height; a kitchen also gets plain standard-height (Outlet group) outlets on the wall spaces the cabinets leave free, a bath none, so a bath with a vanity has outlets only over it. A kitchen with no cabinets drawn keeps the old behaviour (counter-height GFCI all around) because doubling every position at two heights is not what Chief does; a bath with no vanity keeps wall GFCI at the Outlet height. Chief states the rules but not the spacing, so the factor two for Fewer is our choice (verify in Chief). `auto_place_outlets` keeps its signature and passes no rules. |
| DM20 | Setup Temporary (DIM-53, round 16): Row Limit is the most gap lines on each side of a selected wall (the nearest always shows, further rows sit 18 in apart along the wall); Reach limits only the rows after the nearest (a gap line to the nearest object is the point of a temporary dimension, so it is never hidden by Reach); a wall kind with none of its side boxes checked is not located; a gap runs to the nearest checked face of the other wall. Both exterior sides are now checked by default (Primary alone would run every gap to the outside of the building). | DM22 | Auto Refresh (DIM-52, round 16): after any change the editor rebuilds an automatic run (Exterior, Room) that was generated before and whose box is checked, and replaces it only when the new run differs from the old, so it is idempotent and part of the edit's undo step. A hand-edited automatic dimension is replaced too, as the manual says. | The manual says delete and replace | 2026-10-09 |

| FM1 | Framing round 16 (brief 29): the Framing Member Defaults, Framing Types, Automatic and Manual Framing Defaults and Structural Member Reporting dialogs are plan-level (stored in the first floor's framing slot as a catalogue record, one undo step per OK), not global saved-defaults lists; they are reached from Build > Framing and from the commands `framing.members`, `framing.types`, `framing.automatic`, `framing.manual`, `framing.reporting`. | RH1 | Round 16 brief 18, Same Roof Height at Exterior Walls (manual pp. 830 and 844): the overhang of a plane of pitch P becomes `default overhang x default pitch / P` (the fascia drop of the default plane), for every plane that meets a plane of another pitch; Wall Specification overhangs are ignored for those planes; an overhang typed in the Roof Plane Specification stays. | RH2 | Round 16 brief 18: the roof builder always joins neighbouring planes at their eave line, so Same Height Eaves places the eave tips at the default plane's seated tip (`plan_roof::seated_eave_elevation`) and Same Roof Height off with Same Height Eaves off behaves the same way (the manual says planes are raised or lowered "relative to the top plate" and does not give the third case's eave height). | RH3 | Heel Height counts only with Trusses and the manual Raise Off Plate / Birdsmouth Cut only with Rafters and Automatic Birdsmouth Cut off; both are added to Raise/Lower All Roof Planes (`RoofSettings::plate_raise`). The automatic birdsmouth keeps the existing seating of the structure's underside on the plate at the wall centerline (the 3D roof builders already did this); the manual's inside-edge seat differs by half the wall thickness times the slope, under an inch. Pivot arithmetic for the plane dialog (`PlaneHeights::with_pitch`) follows the manual: Top of Plate lock pivots about the inside top edge of the plate with the automatic cut and the outside edge without it; the Roof Plane Specification radio buttons that call it are a follow-up (docs/integration-queue.md). |

| OM7 | Extend Existing Roof Over (DW-167, manual pp. 637, 638): the roof builder sees the exterior walls with the stretch behind each such unit replaced by the unit's sections (`Floor::extend_roof_over_units`; Rectangular Roof Over uses the rectangle); the pieces keep the id and roof settings of the wall. The centre line follows the outer face of the unit (half a wall thickness out), which the eave measures from. A unit with a lowered ceiling keeps a hip of its own, as the manual says (`BayUnit::extends_main_roof`). The unit's own roof is skipped whenever the main roof comes down over it. Changing the option needs Build Roof again, as in Chief; the Auto Rebuild signature does not watch it. Verify in Chief. Supersedes the open part of DECISIONS 125. |

The wall behind the unit stays whole. Upper floors get none. A concrete block under the unit is still drawn in 3D when there is no foundation floor yet. Verify in Chief. |
| OM9 | Select, Tab and the depth handle (DW-155, DW-160, DW-161): the hit test lists the openings of a stack in `mull::pick_order`, Tab cycles that list, and with one opening selected and the pointer off the plan Tab acts as Select Next Object. The depth handle is a diamond on the outer face of the selected bay, box or bow; dragging it sets the depth between 6 and 96 in (as the dialog does) and is one undo step named "Change Bay Depth". Single Wall Hole adds one box around the unit to the wall's holes in 3D. Dimension lines locate only Window Level 0 (the automatic exterior set; typed temporary dimensions still measure any opening). |
| OM10 | Defaults per door and window type (DW-154, manual pp. 103, 571, 603): Default Settings > Doors and Windows list a leaf for every type (Double Door, Doorway, Interior and Exterior Sliding Door, Pocket, Bifold, Barn, Fixed and Shower Door; Casement, Fixed, Sliding, Awning and Hopper Window, Bay, Bow and Box Window, Pass-Through, Wall Niche); the Interior and Exterior Hinged Door, the Window and the Garage Door keep their own leaves. A double-click on a Door or Window Tools button opens the type's dialog (the Interior set for a type that has both; the Hinged Door and Window buttons open the dialogs they always had). The dialog is the Specification plus Minimum Separation and the Mulled Unit Defaults for windows, which are the plan's and are written to the window defaults by any window type's OK. Verify in Chief. |
| EB1 | Modifier roles (Round 16 brief 10, Daniel's DT2; manual pp. 190, 192, 193, 237, 253, 260): Alt summons the Alternate edit behavior for the operation (the right button is meant to as well); Ctrl or Cmd overrides snaps, move restrictions and bumping (`snap::overrides`, `PointerEvent::overrides`, `PointerEvent::alternate`); the S key drops the object snaps and keeps the extension anchors; key 1 clears the anchors; Shift restricts the angle snaps to 90 or 45 degrees (`EditingDefaults::restrictive_angle_deg`, Snap Settings "Shift Restricts To"); Esc cancels. This replaces the Alt/Ctrl wording of rows 53, 70 and 77 (those rows stay as history): every tool that read Alt as "suspend the snaps" now reads Ctrl/Cmd (wall, CAD, text, framing, foundation, terrain, details, schedule, materials list, tray ceiling, construction line, library, cabinet resize, opening, dimension, Select handle drags); stairs already had Ctrl for snaps and Alt for the downward direction. A marquee that starts on an object takes Ctrl or Cmd (not Alt) and toggles what it covers, as p. 260 says. Judgment calls: (a) the round 14 copy-drag (row 94, not in the manual) needed Ctrl, which the manual gives to the override (p. 237), so it now takes Ctrl and Alt together (the drag is then unrestricted); (b) the roof Return mode keeps Alt for its boxed return (it is that tool's Alternate); (c) Shift slows the pointer to a quarter while a drawing tool is dragged (`snap::slow_pointer`, main.rs `canvas_event`; not in Select Objects, where Shift marquees), and right-button drags as Alternate are not built (queued). Verify in Chief. |
| EB2 | Edit behaviors (brief 10, manual pp. 252-256; replaces the dominant-axis Alternate of row 13): Alternate keeps the angles beside a dragged polyline corner (the neighbours slide along their edges, `behaviors::reshape_keeping_angles`; a triangle or two-point line cannot keep them and moves the corner alone), moves a wall or a group at the allowed angles (exactly as set by Angle Snaps: the delta turns to the nearest allowed angle and its length rounds to the Snap Unit; with angle snaps off it moves square to the wall), and chains CAD lines by clicking. Move (new `EditBehavior::Move`) makes any resize handle move the whole object. Resize: a polyline corner handle scales in proportion about the opposite bounds corner. Concentric: a polyline or circle handle moves every edge the same distance in Concentric Jump steps (0 uses the Snap Unit; `EditBehaviorSettings::concentric_jump`); the body drag that leaves offset copies stays as Plan Studio's own extra. Fillet is as before; Chamfer and Replicate remain Plan Studio extras beside Chief's six. The global behavior is Default at start and exit (`behaviors::restore_default`); the round 14 rule that leaving Select Objects resets it to Default (S-66) stays as it was, although the manual keeps the choice until the program closes (verify in Chief); held keys summon one for the operation (Z or /, X or ., C, F; Alt+Z or Alt+/ Default; X1 Concentric, X2 Resize), `behaviors::summon`. Connect CAD Segments stays the View flag and is now also in Edit. Open polylines turning an end handle into an arc, the Lock Center override of arcs and Point to Point dimension locating are not built. Verify in Chief. |
| EB3 | Enter Coordinates (brief 10, manual pp. 196-197; S-132): Tab or Enter with nothing typed, while the Wall tool has a start or the Select tool is dragging a move or a wall end, opens the dialog (`dialogs::enter_coordinates`, `Tool::coordinate_origin`). It takes Absolute or Relative to Start with X and Y, or Polar with Distance and Angle (always from the start), remembers the choice, and answers by filling the typed length and angle (`TypedInput::set_polar`) and sending Enter, so each tool's existing typed-entry commit does the work (a bumping rule on type-in movement is therefore the tool's). Tab with nothing typed used to switch to the angle field; that now opens the dialog (the field still switches once something is typed). The CAD tool keeps its own typed line (Enter after the first click) and Input Line/Arc/Point; the dialog is not wired to it. Verify in Chief. |
| EB4 | Arithmetic in number boxes (brief 10, tutorial pp. 244, 280, 283; APP-84, APP-145): `plan_core::calc` evaluates `+ - * /` between lengths, fractions and plain numbers, hooked into `parse_length`, so every field that reads lengths gains it. Reading rules: `+`, `*` always operate; `-` operates when spaced on both sides or after an inch mark; `/` operates when spaced or after a foot or inch mark (`5/8` is a fraction, `12'-6` and `12-6` stay feet-inches); `*` and `/` bind tighter; a plain number scales a length and a length over a length is a plain ratio; a lone plain number or one added to a length is read in the field's unit. A spaced `12' - 6` therefore now means 12 feet less 6 inches (it used to read 12'6"). The typed length of the Wall and Select tools and the typed angle accept it too. Fields that parse with `parse_ft_in` directly (a few dimension and tempdim entries) do not yet. |
| EB5 | Snap settings (brief 10, manual pp. 122, 149, 150, 190-194; S-123, S-125, S-127, DS-47): Extension anchors are the endpoint, midpoint and quadrant points the pointer rested on, newest last, up to Objects in History (default 6); with the Extension snap on, a point near the horizontal or vertical line through an anchor snaps onto it (`snap::anchor_extension`). Additional Angles (with their opposing angles) join the increment's set (`snap::allowed_angle_set`); Shift swaps the set for 90 or 45. The Angle Snap Grid (Snap Settings, off by default) fans the allowed angles out as faint rays from the last point of a wall or CAD line (`snap::draw_angle_rays`); the small hatch marks at the Snap Unit are not drawn. Not built: Always Snap Walls on Allowed Angles, snap unit per view kind, reference grid size and dots, tangent/perpendicular extension lines from an anchor. |
| ST16-1 | Ramp Specification Options and Tread Surface (round 16, CB-191, CB-192): the manual lists Open Underneath, Max Thickness, Has Tread Surface, Tread Overhang and Tread Thickness but not how they shape the solid, so: Open Underneath (default on, the round 15 look) leaves the slab hanging `slab_thickness` under the surface; off fills the space to the floor, never deeper than Max Thickness (default 12 inches, never less than the slab). The tread surface is a separate plate whose top is the walking height (the slab gives way beneath it, so landings still meet at the same height); the overhang reaches past both sides and both ends along the slope. Stored in `StairParams::ramp` (`RampOptions`, serde default so older plans load unchanged). Verify in Chief. |
| ST16-2 | Section length (round 16, CB-107): a dragged stair or ramp run is held between 6 inches and 100 feet (`plan_stairs::clamp_section_run`) when the tool builds the stair; the specification dialog does not yet refuse a typed run beyond them because the run follows the tread depth and rise. Landings that split a longer climb belong to the sections work (brief 21). |

Camera-view layer sets stay with the Camera Defaults, as the manual says. Verify in Chief. | As described | 2026-10-10 |

| LS4 | Drawing Group of CAD (round 16, CD2 correction): the code already starts CAD in group 21 (behind Walls at 50, `drawing_group::KINDS`) and Text in 80, as the manual (p. 354) says; CD2 above still lists "CAD and Text 80" and is superseded by this row. | WR1 | Off-angle icon range (round 16, brief 14, W-132): the manual does not say how far off an allowed angle a wall may be and still carry the Off Angle icon. A wall is off angle when it is between 0.05 and 5 degrees from the nearest allowed angle (each allowed angle also allows its opposite; with an empty list, the nearest multiple of the angle increment). A wall further off is drawn on purpose. Constants `OFF_ANGLE_MIN_DEG` / `OFF_ANGLE_MAX_DEG` in plan-core `wall_repair.rs`. Parity W-132 and W-133: verify in Chief. | Judgment call | 2026-10-10 |
| WR2 | Connect Walls reach (round 16, brief 14, W-136): the manual says "walls whose ends are close are connected" without a distance. Connect Walls uses four times the automatic connect distance (24 in at least, `CONNECT_WALLS_REACH`) and works only between the two named walls, so a gap the automatic connection left open can be closed. The Auto Connect lock (`WallFlags.lock_start` / `lock_end`) stops a locked end from snapping and from attracting other walls. Verify in Chief. | Judgment call | 2026-10-10 |
| WR3 | Acute junctions (round 16, brief 14, W-132): walls that leave a junction less than 60 degrees apart end square instead of mitered (`joins::ACUTE_JOIN_DEG`, `is_acute_join`); exactly 60 degrees still miters. The older 4x-thickness miter limit stays as a second guard. Verify in Chief. | As described | 2026-10-10 |
| WR4 | Align With Wall Above / Below (round 16, brief 14, W-145): the selected wall slides sideways until its main-layer edge lines up with the nearest main-layer edge of the parallel wall (under 5 degrees apart, centerlines within 24 in) that overlaps it most on the next floor up or down; walls joined to its ends follow. The manual says "outer edges"; the nearest pair of edges is used so the move is the smallest one. Verify in Chief. | Judgment call | 2026-10-10 |
| SV1 | Bearings (round 16, brief 06, CAD-109, CAD-134): `plan_core::bearing`. Plan north is +Y (`NORTH_DEG` 90 as a math angle). A quadrant bearing is N or S, an angle up to 90 (degrees with optional minutes and seconds, any of space or the marks) and E or W; the four due directions are the bare letters N, E, S, W (Chief's text for them is unknown; verify in Chief). An azimuth runs clockwise from north; typed with an `Az` prefix it is read as one in any style, and a bare number is an azimuth only under the Azimuth style. Every angle field of the CAD typed input accepts all of these, plus degrees with `°`. Seconds may carry decimals; DMS output rounds to the style's decimals and carries into minutes. | As built | 2026-10-10 |
| SV2 | Number Style (round 16, brief 06, PR-30, PR-31): `bearing::NumberStyle` holds the length format, angle format, pitch style (rise in 12 or degrees) and custom length/area/volume units (`value = base * multiplier`). It is held per session in `tools::cad::survey` (thread-local); storing it in the plan and the Preferences / Default Settings button are open (integration queue). The typed Length field now reads a bare number in the style's unit (decimal feet reads feet; the default feet-inches style still reads inches), and the typed Angle field starts with the cursor direction in the Angle Style, blank when the cursor is on the start. | As built | 2026-10-10 |
| SV3 | Current Point and Next (round 16, brief 06, CAD-112, CAD-113): the Current Point is the last point typed or created by New CAD Point / Line (`survey::current_point`), kept in a thread-local; Delete removes temporary points in reverse order through `survey::delete_current_point` (not yet bound to the key). Next in Input Line is the Connect CAD Segments chain: after Enter the typed fields reopen on the line's end. Place Point, Input Point and Point Marker differ only by input (click or typed) and the circle on a marker. | As built | 2026-10-10 |
| SV4 | Show Length / Show Angle (round 16, brief 06, CAD-118): `CadAttrs.labels` (`CadLabels`: length, angle, radius, all angles, reverse angle) draws the length centered above and the angle (or `R radius` on an arc) centered below each edge, in the Number Style, from `draw_cad_styled`; labels stay horizontal for now (the rotation is computed in `EdgeLabel` but not applied). The labels are live because they are formatted at draw time. | As built | 2026-10-10 |
| SV5 | Convert to Terrain Perimeter (round 16, brief 06, CAD-134): `survey::convert_to_terrain_perimeter` takes the chain of lines in order, refuses a chain that is open by more than half an inch, drops the repeated last corner and sets the terrain perimeter in one undo step; the CAD lines stay. | As built | 2026-10-10 |
| CS6 | Orthographic Full, Floor and Framing Overviews and the four Isometric Views (round 16, brief 32, C-15): the same orbit camera in parallel projection (`Camera::parallel`, `make_parallel`; zoom is the visible height). An Isometric View looks from SW, SE, NE or NW, 45 degrees round and 30 degrees down (the conventional angles, not the true-isometric 35.3 degrees). The Framing Overview is the Full Overview in parallel projection until a framing-only scene exists. They are under 3D > Create Orthographic View. | CS7 | Depth Cue (round 16, brief 32, C-140): on a section or elevation drawing the fog is shown as line weight. A line `d` inches behind the nearest drawn line has fog 0 up to Start, the Fog Opacity from End on and a linear ramp between (a step at Start when Keep Start/End in Sync is on); under a third of fog keeps the weight, under two thirds steps down one class, more is Light (`plan_elevation::Options::depth_cue`, `hlr::cue_weight`). Text, dimensions and CAD are not touched. | Drawings have three line weights, no transparency | 2026-10-10 |
| CS8 | Cross Section Lines and Point Markers (round 16, brief 32, C-131): `plan_elevation::cross_section_lines` reads the cut regions of the drawing and gives every object cut in a piece of the plane four lines (left face 0, right face 1, bottom 2, top 3; plus 4 per piece after the first), found again by `CutRef`. A dimension end within 1.5 in of a line is moved onto it and a Point Marker is added (`attach_cut_markers`, called by whichever tool adds a dimension to a view); a marked end follows the line when the view is drawn again. The lines are drawn as thin coloured lines of the view only (`Drawing::styled`), never in the layout or the DXF, as the manual says; the Point Markers are annotations and go to layout. The "Cross Section Lines" layer (locked, on) and "CAD, Clip Lines" layer (off) are made with the first camera (`ensure_camera_layer`). | Manual pp. 1167, 1173 | 2026-10-10 |
| CS9 | Cross Section Slider (round 16, brief 32, C-141): six cutting planes (Left, Right, Front, Back, Top, Bottom), each with a check box, a Position slider and a typed Position measured from the edge of the model that plane reaches first. 3D > Camera View Options > Cross Section Slider opens the dialog in a camera view or overview and keeps it open while the user works; Done saves the planes with the active camera in one undo step (a live overview keeps them for the session). Elsewhere the command still opens the single-plane elevation slider (C-23). Triangles are kept or dropped by centroid, as in the other scene clips. Not carried into CAD Detail or layout, since those draw from the camera's section, not the scene. | Manual pp. 1176, 1177 | 2026-10-10 |
| CS10 | Below Grade (round 16, brief 32, C-153): lines of a section or elevation under the limit are split at it and restyled. The Terrain Perimeter limit is the terrain's elevation at the camera (`grade_height`; the first floor's level without terrain), not the surface along the whole cut line. Override Weight (points) picks the nearest of the three weight classes; Override Style turns any non-solid style into the dashed hidden-line look; Override Color adds a coloured copy of those lines to the view (`Drawing::styled`), so a layout or DXF gets the weight and dashes but not the colour. The list of affected object types is shown for reference only. | Drawings have three line weights | 2026-10-10 |
| CS11 | Overview camera symbols (round 16, brief 32, C-14, update to DECISIONS 166): a saved overview is selectable, editable and copyable in the plan. Its symbol stands at the eye and looks toward the target, with its clip distance as the distance to the target; the saved pose keeps the two heights and `ViewPose::symbol` says the symbol places the rest (`CameraObject::overview_pose`), so moving, aiming, stretching or copying the symbol moves the view. Overviews saved before this keep their pose and their hidden symbol (`symbol` reads false). | Manual pp. 1153, 1157 | 2026-10-10 |
| CS12 | Camera Specification panels (round 16, brief 32): the tabs are the manual's Camera, Positioning, Below Grade, Selected Defaults, Plan Display, Backdrop, Layer and Label (pp. 1186 to 1194). The old Rendering tab is folded into the Camera tab, whose groups follow the manual (General, Rendering, Upscaling, Depth of Field, Lighting, Options). A section or elevation shows the shorter lists of pp. 1195 and 1196. Selected Defaults is the Active Defaults panel (`default_sets::Selected`) for the view; a view that picks what the plan already uses stores nothing and keeps following the plan. | Manual pp. 1186 to 1200 | 2026-10-10 |
| CS13 | Hide Camera-Facing Exterior Walls (round 16, brief 32, C-151): with the box on, an Exterior wall whose outer face looks toward a camera outside every room is left out of the view with its doors and windows, and the Interior walls of an Attic floor with them (`camera_view::facing`). An overview uses its saved eye; an unsaved overview hides nothing. Cabinets and symbols mounted on those walls are not hidden yet. | Manual pp. 1189, 1205 | 2026-10-10 |
| CS14 | Plan Display and Layer (round 16, brief 32, C-119, C-156, C-158): Display as Callout is the existing `callout.show`. Placement Center, Left Side, Right Side, Both Sides (two callouts) or Custom (Offset from Center); Callout Label overrides the view number; Callout Size is the diameter; Text Below Line typed shows under the bubble and Automatic shows the layout sheet reference that was already there; arrows are drawn on the clip plane line for the side placements; Cross Section Line Style and Weight draw the plan line unless By Layer. Camera Symbol Size scales the glyph and Show Field of View Indicators hides the cone until selected. Display on All Floors is `Project::cameras_on`. Show Camera Focal Point and FOV Indicator Length are stored only. A camera symbol is hidden with the layer its Layer panel names; its Drawing Group number is stored only. | CS15 | Camera panel options (round 16, brief 32, C-146 to C-151): Show Color off draws a view in the grey of each colour; Show Watermark paints the plan's text watermark over the 3D view (pictures print only); Ambient Occlusion scales the look's occlusion (the slider's middle keeps it); Use Sunlight off switches the sun and its shadows off; Clip Surfaces Within is the near plane (always for an eye-level camera, for an overview once it differs from 2 in); Maximum Number truncates the plan's lights; Depth of Field sets the Ray Trace lens (aperture 24 in over the F-Stop, focus distance as typed). | CS16 | Auto Elevations one side at a time (round 16, brief 32, C-114): Front (South, bottom of the plan on screen), Back (North), Left (West) and Right (East) Elevation under 3D > Create Auto Elevations make that one elevation; running it again updates the camera of that name as All Elevations already does, so there is no "2" numbering. Verify in Chief. | Manual p. 1151 | 2026-10-10 |
| ST21-1 | Merged sections (round 16, brief 21, CB-134, CB-143): the manual says merged subsections can differ in tread depth, but the stair solver and 3D layout take one depth per flight. Merging two straight flights in this build makes one straight section whose run (both runs plus the gap between them) is spread over every tread, so the depth is the same throughout, as if Ignore Subsection Boundaries were always on; the junction tread is made from the gap (a gap of one tread depth keeps the depth unchanged). `StairParams::subsections` keeps the tread counts per subsection for the 1-1, 1-2 numbers and the table. Curved sections do not merge yet (`MergeError::Shape`). Verify in Chief. | Manual pp. 784-785, 789 | 2026-10-10 |
| ST21-2 | Make Best Fit (round 16, brief 21, CB-159, CB-160): the Best Fit riser is the riser height nearest 6 3/4 inches that divides the rise exactly (the counts either side of rise / 6.75; on a tie the higher count). For a staircase of several sections the total rise of all sections is divided into that count: every section gets the Best Fit riser height, the other sections keep their riser counts and the selected section takes the difference, so its length changes by whole treads at its current tread depth. The command moves the end its Lock End leaves free; the Edit button uses Lock Bottom. The dialog's button sees only its own section and fits that section's own rise. Downward stairs are not extended to terrain yet. | Manual p. 803 | 2026-10-10 |
| ST21-3 | Lock Top / Lock Bottom and tread modes (round 16, brief 21, CB-162, CB-143): the two lock flags already stored (tread depth, number of treads) are the Advanced Options radio group (Automatic Treads, Lock Tread Depth, Lock Number of Treads, No Change; both flags on reads as No Change and refuses a length change). Lock Top keeps the section's top; the section and everything below it (found by height) move back along its direction by the growth, Lock Bottom moves the top and everything above it forward. The Lock End choice is a dialog-only setting (default Lock Bottom); Chief's rule that clicking near the bottom end selects Lock Top is `LockEnd::from_click`, not yet used by the picker. Verify in Chief. | Manual pp. 803-804 | 2026-10-10 |
| ST21-4 | Landing Auto Adjust (round 16, brief 21, CB-138, CB-139): `landing_auto_height` and `landing_auto_thickness` (both on). Auto thickness is one riser of the attached section plus floor finish (0: the floor finish is not modelled) or 6 3/4 inches when no section touches the landing; auto height follows the section that arrives, and a landing within 1 inch of an earlier one (lower id) is one riser above it (the riser of its section, 7 1/2 inches with none) and both lose the railing on the shared edges (`EdgeRail::No`). Typing a height or thickness in the Landing Specification turns that Auto Adjust off. Existing landings keep their stored thickness until a section is joined or the dialog is saved. | Manual pp. 787-788 | 2026-10-10 |
| LS5 | Object Layer Properties (round 16, brief 09, LAY-64, LAY-72): the Edit-toolbar button opens a window with the Layer Display Options table cut down to the layers of the selected objects (primary layer first, then secondary), a Show All Layers box, and the properties of the selected rows; edits go to the shown layer set. Secondary layers are a fixed list per object kind and only the ones the plan has are listed: wall (Walls, Layers; Main Layer Only; Through Wall Lines; Brick Ledge Lines; Footings for foundation walls; No Locate for no-locate walls), door and window (their label layer), cabinet (Countertops, Doors & Drawers, Front Indicators, Module Lines, Labels), electrical device (Electrical Connection), room (Rooms, Room Labels). The manual does not list them, so the list is our reading; the three cabinet layers are never created yet (CB-485, the cabinet owner). The camera symbol has no such button, as in the manual. Verify in Chief. | As described | 2026-10-10 |
| LS6 | Wall system layers (round 16, brief 09, LAY-76): named literally as the manual lists them (Walls, Layers; Walls, Main Layer Only; Walls, Through Wall Lines; Footings; Brick Ledge Lines; Walls, No Locate; Walls, Attic). They are system layers (cannot be deleted or merged away) but are not added to a plan on their own, so Daniel's 34 x17 sets and old plans are unchanged: the Wall Layers button and Reset Names add them to the plan and to every layer set (layer lines shown, the rest hidden). A plan without them draws walls as before. Today only two have an effect in the plan: turning "Walls, Layers" off hides the lines between wall layers, and turning "Walls, Main Layer Only" on draws just the main layer; the others are switches for the wall owner to draw on. | As described | 2026-10-10 |
| LS7 | Layer Hider (round 16, brief 09, LAY-73): the fifth tool of the painter family (`PainterMode::LayerHider`, Tools > Layer Painter > Layer Hider). While hovering the status bar names the layer. | As described | 2026-10-10 |
| LS8 | Layer fill (round 16, brief 09, LAY-67): the Fill column and the Fill Style preview use the fill style the plan already keeps per display layer (`FillTarget::Layer` in `styles.fill_assign`), edited through the Fill Style dialog (`fill_style::open_for_layers`). Copy gives the copy the same fill and line style; Delete and Merge drop the fills of the layers that go away and keep the one of the layer that stays. Remove Fill clears it. | As described | 2026-10-10 |
| LS9 | Find Objects on Layer(s) (round 16, brief 09, LAY-62): selects the objects on the chosen layers, going to the floor that has them; with objects on several floors the Select Location window lists the floors with counts (the existing `select_location` dialog gained a `Target::LayerObjects`). Objects on a layer that is off or locked cannot be selected, so the status says to turn the layer on. The manual only says "views"; we list floors because plan views share floors. | As described | 2026-10-10 |
| LS10 | New views read Layer Set Defaults (round 16, brief 09, LAY-70): only when a set was chosen for the kind. A new plan view (Tools > New Plan View) starts with the Floor Plan choice, a CAD detail opened as a tab starts with the CAD Detail choice, and a Reference Display row of this plan that names no layer set uses the Reference Floor choice. The framing, roof, electrical, section and elevation kinds have no view-creation path that takes a layer set yet; their owners call `Project::initial_layer_set(kind)`. | As described | 2026-10-10 |
| LS11 | Drawing group buttons (round 16, brief 09, LAY-74): the Edit toolbar and Edit > Drawing Group list Send to Back, Send Backward, Bring Forward and Bring to Front for any selection (the manual's View Drawing Group Edit Tools). Bring Forward and Send Backward move every selected object exactly one group (LS4), one undo step each; an object already at the end of the range is left alone and the command says so. The manual's "back of its current group" part of Send Backward (re-ordering inside a group) is not built: inside a group the draw order is still the order of creation. Verify in Chief. | Manual p. 218 | 2026-10-10 |
| SV6 | Number Style is part of the plan (round 16, brief 06, PR-30, PR-31, DS-47): `Project::number_style` (skipped when it is the factory style) holds it, one undo step (`survey::store_number_style`); `EditorContext::refresh` calls `survey::on_refresh`, which brings the style in force up to date whenever the plan's own style changes (opening, New, Undo, Redo) and leaves a session-only `set_number_style` alone until then. The Number Style dialog (`dialogs/number_style.rs`) opens from Preferences > Unit Conversions and from Default Settings > General Plan Defaults, and from CAD > Survey Entry. The Default Settings button edits the plan's own style (the template carries it with My Template), not a separate stored default. | As built | 2026-10-10 |
| SV7 | CAD Defaults lay over the Number Style for the live labels (round 16, brief 06, CAD-118): "Displayed Line Length Format", "Accuracy" and "Display Line Angles As" of Default Settings > CAD > General CAD change the Show Length / Show Angle labels only when set away from the built-in value; left at the built-in value the labels follow the Number Style (`survey::overlay_cad_defaults`). Bearing reads as Quadrant Bearing. The labels are rotated along their edge and kept upright (text angle between -90 and 90 degrees); All Angles is stored but every edge of a polyline is labelled, since the manual's "selected edge" has no counterpart here. | As described | 2026-10-10 |
| SV8 | New CAD Line, New CAD Arc and Move Point dialogs (round 16, brief 06, CAD-8, CAD-112, CAD-134): CAD > Survey Entry opens New CAD Line and New CAD Arc; double-click (Open Object) on a temporary point opens Move Point (`main.rs open_spec`, `survey::temporary_point_of`). OK draws and closes, Next draws and restarts at the end (a line after a line, an arc after an arc, heading the way the last ended); each is one undo step. New CAD Arc follows the manual p. 337: Start Direction or Chord Direction, Radius with Left (counter-clockwise) or Right curve, then Arc Angle, Arc Length or Chord Length (a chord longer than the diameter is refused; the shorter arc is drawn). Delete with nothing selected removes the latest temporary point; selecting a temporary point makes it the Current Point (`survey::on_refresh`). The New CAD Point dialog is not a separate window: Input Point types its X and Y inline, and Move Point does the numeric move. | As built | 2026-10-10 |
| SV9 | Line and Arc Specification locks (round 16, brief 06, CAD-116): the General tab has Lock radio buttons. Line: Start, End, Center, Length/Angle (manual p. 332): the locked part stays while the length, angle or an end changes, and Length/Angle moves the other end with the one edited; a box the lock freezes is greyed. Arc: Start, End, Center, Arc, Chord (manual p. 339); radius, center and the two angles change the arc under the lock (`dialogs/cad/locks.rs`, tested). The chord length and angle and the start and end directions are shown, not typed; typing them (and the arc length) is open. The default locks are Start for a line (as before) and Center for an arc (as before). "Change Line/Arc with a Lock Chord radius" is read as the Chord lock of the Arc Specification: the radius changes with the two ends held. Verify in Chief. | Partly built | 2026-10-10 |
| SV10 | Disconnect Edges and Hide / Show Selected Edge (round 16, brief 06, CAD-117): two CAD edit tools (CAD > Edit CAD, the Lines flyout family). Disconnect Edges: the clicked edge of an open or closed polyline becomes a line or arc object of its own (selected), the rest stays as one or two polylines with the same look, one undo step; a polyline of one edge is left alone. Hide/Show Selected Edge is one tool that toggles the clicked edge (`CadAttrs::hidden_edges`, drawn by `style.rs`); the Show Selected Edge box of the Polyline Specification and separate Hide and Show buttons are open. A hidden edge is still clickable where it was. | As built | 2026-10-10 |
| SV11 | Negative inches typed without marks (round 16, brief 06): `plan_core::units::parse_length` read `-6` as +6 in the feet-inches and inches styles, because the dash after a feet mark was also stripped from a bare number; a leading minus on a number with no feet mark now negates (`-6`, `-3 1/2`, `-6in`). `12'-6"` is unchanged. | Bug fix | 2026-10-10 |
| HK16 | Hotkey 2: Daniel's file binds `2` to Join Roof Planes (command 231), so the Plan Studio number-key alias "2 = Wall" gives way to it (DT1: Daniel's hotkeys win). 1, 3 and 4 keep their aliases (Select, Door, Window) because his file does not bind them. The s12 test and the named-binding count (144) follow. | Round 16 gate | 2026-10-10 |
| DM25 | Auto Interior Dimensions placement (Daniel's drafting practice, 2026-10-10): the horizontal clear-span string of a room runs left to right through the whole plan at that height, from the inner face of the west exterior wall to the inner face of the east one, locating every interior wall face it crosses; it sits in the upper third of the room (one sixth of the room's clear height below its north face). The vertical string likewise runs through the plan top to bottom and sits in the right third of the room (one sixth of the clear width in from the east face). A room whose upper third (or right third) already holds such a line from another room gets no second line. The opening strings along each wall are unchanged. End marks: the Tick arrow style draws Chief's architectural tick, a short 45-degree stroke, in plan, layout and PDF; the long drafting slash stays "Slash". Choice of the right third over the left is ours; verify Daniel's preference. | As described | 2026-10-10 |
| CS17 | Add Break and Make Parallel / Perpendicular edit buttons on a section camera (round 16 finish, brief 32, C-137): Add Break (selected section) puts a break in the middle of the widest piece, as the dialog button does (`editor/camera_edit.rs`); Make Parallel / Make Perpendicular show when a break handle was last pressed with the Select tool (kept as a thread-local in `camera_edit`, valid only while that camera is the single selection and the index exists) and call `ClipPlane::make_parallel/make_perpendicular`. Chief puts the break at the click; the button has no click, so the middle is used. Verify in Chief. |
